             THE YEAR OF 1925

October--first Beauty Parlor opened--"Comfort Beauty Shoppe" operator was
Mrs. Reuben Johnson.

October--$100,000. fire at Mine

Fall of 1925 the school enrollment was 230

June 4--Outdoor meeting of Ku Klux Klan--it was reported the Klan had a
substantial membership. On their June 11, meeting they had the traditional
burning of the cross.

Population in 1925 was 750, 625 in the incorporated part of town. Twenty new
houses were built during the year.

The first bowling alley was opened by Ted Muhlhauser.

BEULAH WOMAN'S CLUB

The Beulah Woman's Club is a member of the North Dakota Federation of Women's
Clubs. It is a study club. It was organized in 1930 with the following
membership: Mrs. F. Rasmusson, Mrs. L. Rose, Mrs. J. Halbeisen, Mrs. C.
Ellinger, Mrs. A. Gilchrist, Mrs. J. Harold, Mrs. M. Held, Mrs. W. Lane, Mrs.
A. Lang, Mrs. C. Malaise, Mrs. F. Marshall, Mrs. M. Moore, Mrs. I. Neidermeyer,
Mrs. T. Scholz and Mrs. I. Vincent.

The major project of the club through the years has been a public library. The
library was established in February of 1931, and with the cooperation of the
townspeople and other clubs, has grown to fit the sizable room provided for it
in the Beulah City Hall. Since Nov. 1941 it has been a free library.

In the early years Beulah Woman's Club helped with all the health clinics in
town. Later on, in World War II, it earned the money for a nurse's scholarship;
conducted a War Band Drive, and members were the Molly Pitchers one Saturday
night.

Woman's Club helped with the first March of Dimes, (Mary Held--County Chairman
for 25 years)--in the theater collections. It has assisted actively in
"Get-Out-The-Vote" campaigns, especially, for all local improvement projects.
In 1950 it was the Woman's Club that distributed the 15,000 trees donated by
Zap Collieries (during the management of Ralph Richardson), for the
beautification of the vicmity. It collaborated with others to buy a sterilizer
for the Beulah Hospital and later raised money to furnish a room in the
proposed new hospital.

In the field of education, its accomplishments are many and varied. It has
sponsored creative writing among its members, art contests in the local school
(Rae Richardson won a State award) and partially paid the way of several local
high school musicians' trips to the International Music Camp. It applied for
and received the North Dakota State Federation Scholarship for a Beulah School
Senior; has raised money for Band Uniforms and been represented on the District
VII Foley Poem reprint project. From 1948 to 1958 it was the sponsor of the
Junior-Senior School Spring formal.

Besides conducting a survey on Community Improvement, the members have started
a collection of Pioneer Histories and pictures. They have been the Welcome
Wagon good will messengers. Woman's Club arranged an open forum--"Woman and her
Bank" and an informative question and answer public program on cancer in
connection with a high school style show.

Among its other local programs have been home talent plays and a Folk Program
which featured a singing group from the Mandan State Training School.

Perhaps its most far reaching project was the staging of a 2 day Hobby Show in
the '30's. It had exhibitors from all over Mercer County and some from Morton.

As a Federated Club, it has been active in district and state work. Three times
Beulah Woman's Club has been host to District VII at the annual fall
convention, including the district's Golden Jubilee in fall of 1958. In 1933
the Women's trio (Mmes. Lewis, Halbeisen and Held) won the district song
contest. Four local members took part in the Lewis & Clark Pageant staged at
the State Federation's Golden Jubilee Convention in Bismarck, Spring of 1947.
District offices have been held. District Historians--Mrs. M. Moore and Mrs. H.
Dally; District Secretary, Mrs. T. Herman; Dist. President, Mrs. C. Thelander;
State Directors, Mrs. C. Thelander and Mrs. M. Held.

Members: Mrs. L. Aasen, Mrs. A. Abbey, Mrs. D. Barta, Mrs. D. Dettmann, Mrs. R.
Easton, Mrs. J. Herman, Mrs. T. Herman, Mrs. E. Morman, Mrs. J. Power, Mrs. W.
Sayler, Mrs. Q. Seifert, Mrs. F. Stetson and Mrs. C. Thelander, Mrs. E.
Liebelt, Jr. and Mrs. V. Burchill.

        50th Anniversary

BEULAH WOMAN'S CLUB LIBRARY

Beulah Woman's Club Library, a public library, opened Feb. 1, 1931 in the Foyer
of the Roxy Theater. It has always been the main project of the Beulah Woman's
Club since first proposed in 1930. There was cooperation from the townspeople
and other town organizations to collect books and get the project in action.
Since then the library has moved to the school, to the old city hall, to the
Legion hall, to the old city hall and last into the fine room provided for it
by the City of Beulah, when the new hall was built in 1959.

The Woman's Club members take turns serving on the library committee, and many
of them have served as librarians and assistant librarians from time to time.

It was early found that the library worked more efficiently with one member in
charge as librarian.

To begin with, library membership cards were sold each year. This was abandoned
in 1941--since which time it has been a free public library.

There have been many club projects for its support and improvement. Every year
there is a Library Tea in April and a spring rummage sale in May. Book
donations from townspeople are a welcome help.

The library is open on Tuesdays, from 7 to 8 o'clock; Thursdays from 2 to 5
o'clock and Saturdays from 4 to 5 o'clock. Once a month during the winter, on
Saturday morning, Club members take turns conducting a story hour for 4 to 5
year olds.

In addition to its over 3500 books, the library houses Bookmobile books for
Beulah.

THE CLIFFORD McLAUGHLIN TRIPLETS

The Clifford McLaughlin triplets were born Sept. 13, 1941 at Hillcrest Hospital
in Beulah--Dr. Edmund Vinje attending physician. This multiple birth of male
triplets, occurs once in 20,000 births--triplets occur once in 7,901 births.

The Pet Milk Company provided milk gratis for these babies.

The McLaughlins (Mrs. McLaughlin is Herb. Engbrecht's sister) live in Spokane,
Washington, where all three boys have been graduated from college. There are
six other children in the family.

APLIN HOMEMAKERS

Aplin Homemakers named their club after the Aplin Postoffice which was located
in this Community in the early days. It is called Aplin as a gentleman from
Missouri, known as Henry Clay Aplin arrived here in March 1906 and homesteaded
on Section 30 Twp. 143 Range 87. He wrote to Washington D.C. and explained the
need for a postoffice here. So it was started and bore his name. The neighbors
took turns bringing the mail to his place from Nisbet. Nisbet was located two
miles north and seven miles east of where the Aplin school house now stands.
There was also a school house and store at Aplin.

Aplin Homemakers Club was organized in 1947 and the first officers and charter
members are:

        The Clay Butte Club

Aplin transferred to Mercer in 1948-1949 as it was the trading center for most
members and more convenient for business transactions.

This club is really a revival of the former Clay Butte Club which was organized
in 1935 and disbanded in 1944.

The Charter Members of Clay Butte Club were: Mrs. Wm. Walsh, Mrs. Floyd
Erickson, Mrs. Arthur Erickson, Mrs. John Scheidt, Mrs. Henry Conrath, Mrs.
Paul Rettke, Mrs. Otto Rettke and Mrs. Elphie Strom. The first officers were
Mrs. Henry Conrath, president and Mrs. Paul Rettke, Sec. and Treas.

The Club has participated in many phases of Homemakers work. They sponsored the
Christmas Party for the Knife River 4-H Club for five years. Donates annually
to the Red Stocking Fund, Crippled Children's Home, Peace Garden, Exchange
Student Program and has donated miscellaneous gifts to the City Hall and
Nursing Home.

Each year the Club sponsors a Smorgasbord for the members and their families.

There are seventeen members and the meetings are held monthly on te second
Thursday. The present officers are:

THE BEULAH HOMEMAKERS CLUB

        Back Row--Mrs. H. Scherbinske, Alice Abbey, Mrs. E. Liebelt III, Mrs.
        E. Liebelt Sr. Front Row--Mrs. A. Hanson, Mrs. E. Sailer, Mrs. R.
        Easton.

In 1922 Mr. Grover Poe local county agent, recognizing the need of club work
for women, was instrumental in organizing the Beulah Homemakers Club. Mrs. Poe
was installed as the president. This club group was active through the 1920's.
Because it was the only homemakers club in Beulah the membership was large,
this made it difficult to hold all the meetings in the homes of members.
Meetings were held in local church basements.

On February 21, 1935 the Beulah Homemakers Club was reorganized by O. K. Cline,
County Agent. Several new clubs were formed. Mercer County now had eight clubs.
Others were: Stanton, Mannhaven, Hazen, Chaffee Row, Conrath, and Hazen Women's
Club.

The first meeting of the Beulah Homemakers Club was held on March 14, 1935, the
officers and members were: Pres.--Mrs. C. B. Heinemeyer, Vice President--
Mrs. Irv Vincent, Secretary-treasurer--Miss Amber Davidson, members; Mrs. J.
A. Baldwin, Mrs. M. J. Bury, Mrs. Steve Davidson, Mrs. B. Heitzman, Mrs. A.
Gilchrist, Mrs. F. C. Tysver, Mrs. Joe Drashil, Mrs. G. Helvik, Mrs. C. M.
Iverson, Mrs. D. Klein, Mrs. Walter Johnson, Mrs. Ira Miller, Mrs. I. J.
Niedermeyer, Mrs. J. W. Goetz, Mrs. L. G. Rose, Mrs. Clyde Nolan, Mrs. C. V.
Slavick, Mrs. A. Fischer, Mrs. Dorothy Barlow, Mrs. R. Ehlert, Mrs. Susie Mott.

Club meetings were held the third day of each month, dues were 5 cents each
month. Focal point of the club year was achievement day. Each club was
represented by its own delegate. Members enjoyed the companionship of the
monthly meetings and planned activities. The annual Christmas party was a
festive event. Now as then, the extension service offers a well rounded program
of planned lessons. Club work fuses to create a group who achieve satisfaction
in "lessons learned" and "fun" times. Club dues of one dollar a year are
current. Meetings consist of a lesson and social hour. Annual events include
holiday parties and participation in the county wide "Holiday Fair".

1964 officers and members: President--Mrs. Robert Easton, Vice President--
Mrs. Jack Fiergola, Secretary-treasurer--Mrs. Joe Stewart, Program Chairman
--Mrs. Alice Abbey, Mrs. Al Hanson, Mrs. Emanuel Liebelt, Mrs. E. Liebelt Jr.,
Mrs. Emil Sailer, and Mrs. Herb Scherbinske.

        Top--Left to Right: Mmes. J. Thompson, T. Herman, J. Power, C.
        Thelander, W. Sayler, L. Aasen. Bottom--Left to Right: Q. Seifert,
        D. Dettmann, R. Affeldt, E. Morman, G. Koppi.

BEULAH MOTHER'S CLUB

Beulah Mother's Club was organized in September 1935 at the W. Esterly home.
Twelve young mothers formed a social and child study group which has met
continuously since then, on the third Thursday of each month. Motherhood is a
prerequisite for membership. The original number of members has been enlarged
from 12 to 13.

Mrs. Esterly was the first president. The Charter Members were: Mrs. H. J.
Halbeisen, Mrs. E. Shirley, Mrs. P. Keogh, Mrs. W. Esterly, Mrs. B. Webber,
Mrs. T. Carrignan, Mrs. A. Lang, Mrs. S. Frank, Mrs. T. Herman, Mrs. A. Roedel,
Mrs. O. Swanson and Mrs. S. Bacal.

In 1948 the club affiliated with the Homemakers. It retained its particular
identity as a Mother's Club. The members at the time of affiliation with
Homemakers were: Mrs. T. Carrignan, Mrs. W. Esterly, Mrs. D. Buscher, Mrs. T.
Herman, Mrs. H. McBride, Mrs. E. Morman, Mrs. Owens, Mrs. C. Thelander, Mrs. T.
Wolf, Mrs. A. Wolf, and Mrs. R. Vinje.

The program was originally a study of child behavior, based in part on Parent's
Magazine. With membership in the Homemakers organization, the program scope
broadened to include other phases of homemaking.

Mother's Club is not a money-making organization, but has backed such
worthwhile projects as the purchase of Uniforms for the Beulah High School
Band; nursery curtains and kimonas for the hospital; the entertainment of an
IFYE student from Norway. It contributes to the International Peace Garden and
the Crippled Children's Home, as well as other worthwhile causes, when the
occasion arises.

Annually, Mother's Club has been the sole sponsor of a summer kindergarten.
This is its special project. the first kindergarten was heldin the summer of
1939, with Miss Abbie Tyrrell as teacher. Miss Tyrrell taught the kindergar-
ten for the first five years. The kindergarten is now teaching the second
generation of one charter member--Jerome Herman attended the first
kindergarten; Renee, his daughter, attended kindergarten summer of 1963.

Another accomplishment of which Beulah Mother's Club is proud, was obtaining
Miss Marta Kaasik as a full time piano, organ and voice teacher for Beulah and
surrounding communities.

Socially, the calendar has always, annually, had a children's Christmas party
as the main event. For years, Santa Claus was a regular visitor at the parties.
He was sometimes a mother (Buscher and Herman); later Santa was recruited from
the teen age group--Esterlys, Mormans, Herman and Thelanders, taking turns. The
party entertamment has been varied--one year we had a real old fashioned sleigh
ride. After Miss Kaasik came to teach music, she was a regular guest at the
Christmas party. During this period, the children presented programs for the
mothers. The social calendar has broadened to include a parent's pot-luck
Christmas supper during the Hohdays.

THE FRIENDLY HOMEMAKERS

LADIES GUILD HOMEMAKERS CLUB 1948-1964

        Top Row--Left to right: Mrs. G. Zeller, Mrs. E. Heihn, Mrs. E. Lang,
        Mrs. T. Voegele, Mrs. R. Heihn, Mrs. F. Younker. Back Row--Left
        to right: Mrs. C. Jacober, Mrs. T. Heihn, Mrs. H. Hildebrand, Mrs. H.
        Heine, Mrs. G. Younker, Mrs. E. Hilz.

The Ladies Guild Homemakers Club was organized June 29, 1948 under the
direction of Wayne W. Owens, County Extension Agent.

Officers elected were: Mrs. Ted Voegele, president; Mrs. Emil Heihn,
vice-president; Mrs. Robert Schwede, sec.-treas., and Mrs. Reinhold Heihn,
completing the membership.

The purpose of this club is to study and put into practice the best information
on various phases of homemaking, and to promote better community life.

The Ladies Guild meets the third Thursday of each month. Our programs consist
of all major and miscellaneous lessons as submitted by the North Dakota
Extension Service, and participate in the annual county Achievement Day. We
have helped organize two other Homemaker Clubs in the community.

Contributions of our club include gifts to the Joachim Memorial Home, the
Beulah Community Nursing Home, Peace Garden, 4-H Clubs and handicapped
children. Some of our members have been active 4-H leaders throughout the
years. Funds for our club are raised by means of monthly dues and occasional
bake sales.

Social activities include an annual family picnic and a Christmas party.
Members exchange gifts on their birthdays and at Christmas through Secret
Sisters.

The Ladies Guild has its membership restricted to a maximum of 15 members.

In 1952 we were bereaved by the loss of one of our members, Bernice Erickson.

The present officers are: Mrs. Ted Heihn, pres.; Mrs. G. Zeller, v-pres. and
program chairman; Mrs. F. Younker, sec. and Mrs. E. Heihn, treas. Other members
are the Mmes. T. Voegele, R. Heihn, H. Heine, C. Jacober, H. Hildebrand, E.
Hilz, G. Younker, H. Hafner and E. Lang.

NORTHERN STAR HOMEMAKER'S CLUB

        Back Row--Mrs. Otto Kessler, Mrs. E. Voegele, Mrs. P. Becker. Front
        Row--Mrs. C. Sailer, Mrs. R. Raszler, Mrs. T. Boeckel.

Mrs. Phillip Becker and Mrs. Christ Sailer were instrumental in organizing the
"Northern Star" in May of 1952. They invited some of their neighbors to join
them at the Becker home. Mrs. Herbert Tesky, who was a member of a Homemaker
Club, was invited to explain the purpose and objectives of a Homemaker Club to
these prospective members. There was interest shown, so another meeting was
scheduled at the Christ Sailer home, at which time County Extension Agent, Tom
Martindale assisted in organizing this new club.

Officers elected at this organizational meeting were: Mrs. Jake Sailer,
president; Mrs. Phillip Becker, vice-president; Mrs. Gottlieb Weidrick,
secretary-treasurer and Mrs. Christ Sailer, program chairman. Other charter
Members include Mrs. Reinhold Raeszler, Mrs. Otto Kessler, Mrs. Emil Sailer,
Mrs. Andrew Sailer, Mrs. Fred Kessler and Mrs. Carl Schnaidt. As with all
clubs, some discontinued membership and others have joined. As of now, they are
seven, and membership includes four Charter Members who are Mrs. Christ Sailer,
Mrs. Reinhold Raeszler, Mrs. Phillip Becker and Mrs. Otto Kessler.

Their social calendar each year includes a family picnic in August and a
Christmas holiday dinner. Their secret pal for the year is revealed at this
dinner; treats are provided for their children and an exchange of gifts among
the men.

They have a special noteworthy project, in that they contribute food-stuff
treats at Christmas and Easter to the residents of the Beulah Community Nursing
Home and the Joachim Memorial Home. They also donate to the March of Dimes,
Children's Homes, etc.

The Northern Star "shines" the first Tuesday of each month. In the winter
months, members are accompanied to meetings by their husbands and family. The
men enjoy an evening of visiting or game of Pinochle. After the business
meeting and program, they all continue their social hour.

Present officers and members are: Mrs. Reinhold Raeszler, president; Mrs. Otto
Kessler, vice president; Mrs. Christ Sailer, secretary; Mrs. Tom Boeckel,
treasurer; Mrs. Eugene Voegele, program chairman; Mrs. Phillip Becker, card
sending and Mrs. Ray Mohl.

At our regular meeting in April, 1964, service bars for two years of service
were awarded the members.

A few of our members have moved or for other unavoidable reasons have not been
in a position to continue their volunteer services. Membership in the
Organization is a required 50, or more, hours of volunteer service each year,
which we mostly direct to the needs of the elderly people in our county, with a
special service to the shut-ins and the Beulah Community Nursing Home and the
Joachim Memorial Home, and are also subject to call in all disasters and
emergencies.

BEULAH FARMERS UNION ELEVATOR COMPANY

In 1917 the town of Beulah and its businessmen were at a standstill. The
farmers were taking their grain to neighboring towns, because of the large
margins in prices paid for grain. Therefore Beulah people were losing the
farmers trade.

Beulah businessmen, especially Beulah banker P.S. Chaffee, and about sixty-five
community farmers bought stock in and built an elevator they called, "The
Beulah Farmers Elevator Company". The first board of directors consisted of:
Peter Boeckel, Joseph Zeigler, Carl Stephens, David Keller and Jacob Hafner.
The Articles of Incorporation were filed on June 29, 1917.

The exact date the building was completed and opened for business is not
available, but it must have been the fall of 1917. The older generation
reminiscing, at the elevator, talk of the first months the elevator was in
operation. They remember how grain prices became more uniform and farmers
started doing business in Beulah. The town seemed to come alive and started to
grow.

There are no records in existence of business done in the 1920's and 1930's,
but the 1930's "the depression and drought years' were hard on the elevator, as
on everything else. By the end of the 1930's, the business was in the red and
seemed to be heading for bankruptcy.

Being in financial difficulty, on August 5, 1939 the Board of Directors decided
to lease the elevator to The Farmers Union Grain Terminal Association of St.
Paul, Minnesota At a special stockholders meeting held on September 16, 1939
the stockholders voted to reorganize a new elevator company. On January 23,
1940 the deal was completed. One hundred and four farmers bought stock financed
through Federal Security Administration at $100.00 par value to pay off the old
company. The Board of Directors at that time were. August Anderson, President;
Ernest R. Hafner, Secretary; Joseph Zeigler, John Tesky and Henry Conrath,
Directors.

The first annual stockholders meeting of the new company was held on May 18,
1940. Elected officers for a permanent Board of Directors at this time were:
Henry Conrath, President; Joe Zeigler, Vice President; Ernest R. Hafner,
Secretary; Victor Strom and Gus Buchfinck, Directors.

The warehouse capacity in 1939 was 35,000 bushels. In 1941 a wooden annex was
added and the capacity increased to 70,000 bushels. 1951 brought a need for
more storage space and four concrete tanks were constructed by North Star
Construction Company of St. Paul, Minnesota. This increased warehouse capacity
by 80,000 bushels. Again in 1958, due to increased business, additional space
was required and a contract was given Bismarck Building Systems to construct a
Butler Building with a 87,000 bushel capacity. The first wooden annex
constructed in 1941 began to deteriorate and had to be condemned. Therefore a
contract was given Hogenson Construction Company of Minneapolis for six
concrete tanks with a storage capacity of 110,000 bushels, bringing the total
capacity this date to 312,000 bushels.

The old office building, being inadequate, was demolished and a new office was
constructed in 1957 by Chase Construction Company of Hazen.

The volume of business since the reorganization amounts to 5,110,309 bushels of
cash grain plus the CCC grain. Local earnings to the patrons is $452,696.00 of
which $262,807.64 was paid out in cash dividends to the patrons. The balance of
$189,888.36 is the balance of stock credit to the patrons.

The total dividends received from GTA during this time is $109,485.66 of which
$43,031.63 was paid in cash to the patrons. The balance of $66,454.03 is stock
credits to the patrons. The total assets today are $567,006.61.

The present Board of Directors includes: John Kusler, President; Adolf
Anderson, Vice President; Julius Orth, Secretary-Treasurer; Bill Binder and
Thomas Boeckel, Directors.

Though there have been many changes in the construction of the elevator the
personnel have remained quite permanent. Edward Vasichek came to Beulah to
manage the elevator when it was reorganized in 1939 and remained until 1947, at
which time, John Reinhardt then second man was made manager and has filled that
position since. Second men since 1947 have included Leo Zeigler, Leon Walz,
Gottlieb Schmidt, Edwin Bauer and the present second man John Koenig who has
been employed since 1956.

It is the teamwork between patrons, directors and management that makes the
Beulah Farmers Union Elevator an outstanding cooperative. It is this
cooperation that insures its continued success.

Farm families around Beulah and the community of Beulah have a right to be
proud of their cooperative elevator.

HISTORY OF THE CHURCH OF GOD OF BEULAH

Founder and Organizer of the Beulah Church

        The present Church of God built in 1952

        Rev. Paul H. Walker

The Church of God, Beulah, North Dakota, had its beginning in Golden Valley,
North Dakota, in the Elmer Walker home, in 1919, with 53 members. It was
organized by the Rev. G. T. Stargel and Rev. William Hance. Rev. Stargel
pastored the church for quite some time, after which Rev. Charles Cook became
the pastor.

In 1925 the work began to progress and grow, so that this membership was moved
to a country church, north of Beulah, known as the Ree Church of God. In 1930 a
branch church was organized in Beulah, a building was moved in and remodeled on
the east end of main street. It was during this time, that Rev. Paul H. Walker
became the first pastor in this church, and later organized the church. The
Rev. Charles Bowen, was the pastor placed there later. The clerk at that time
was Court Foster.

In 1952 the desires grew for a new modern brick Church, plans were made,
resulting in the erection of the present church, now located on 3rd street,
Beulah. The Rev. Milton Sandberg served the church while the basement was
built, and it was during the time that Rev. E. E. Coleman, was pastor that the
church was completed. The Board of Directors at that time was: The Clerk,
Reinhold Reich; Trustees, Emanual Becker, Reinhold Reich, Albert Pfenning.

The pastors who served the Church of God were: Paul H. Walker, (first pastor
and organizer), Charles Bowen, Eugene Inches, Ralph Brostrom, James Reesor,
Oscar Beckman, E. E. Coleman, M. L. Lowe, Anton Meyers, Milton Sandberg, E. E.
Coleman, N. B. Wills, Bernard Knispel, Arnold Erikson, and the present pastor,
M. D. Haar. The Board of Directors at this time is: Clerk, Albert Pfenning;
Trustees, Leo Sayler, Otto Pfenning, and Art Boeckel.

CHURCH OF GOD DIRECTORS

        Left to right: Milbert D. Haare, Pastor; Leo Sayler, Albert Pfenning,
        Clerk; Otto Pfenning, Trustee; Art Boeckel, Trustee.

THE CHURCH OF GOD WILLING WORKERS BAND

        Front Row--Left to right: Mrs. Christ Jose, Mrs. Art Boeckel, Mrs.
        Wm. Schulz, Mrs. Albert Pfenning, Mrs. Milbert Haare, Mrs. Henry
        Pfenning, Mrs. Otto Pfenning. Back Row--Left to right: Mrs. Joseph
        Walker, Mrs. Reinhold Reich, Mrs. Henry Will, Mrs. Victor Walker, Mrs.
        Albert Boeckel, Mrs. Otto Kusmenko, Mrs. Herbert Buechler, Mrs. Ervin
        Boeckel, Mrs. Richard Pfenning.

The Ladies Willing Workers Band was organized on September 27, 1953, during the
pastorate of the Rev. N. B. Wills.

The purpose of this organization is to assist and aid in the development and
maintenance of the church, and to create interest and good will in every
possible way for the general welfare of the congregation. Their activities have
consisted of donations to the church funds; serving meals for special
occasions, such as weddings, funerals, etc.; donations given both to home and
foreign missions; and visitations made to the sick and shut-in.

They meet in the church once a month. Devotional periods are observed, business
is transacted, and the remainder of the time is taken up discussing the
undertaking of new projects.

The present officers are: President, Mrs. Ethyl Pfenning; Vice-President, Mrs.
Geraldine Haar; Secretary and Treasurer, Mrs Lenora Schulz.

HISTORY OF CONCORDIA LUTHERAN CHURCH

The organizing of Concordia Lutheran Church was a slow process, as in most
beginnings. In the early 1930's and 1940's a small group of people held church
in their homes, schoolhouses and in halls, whenever convenient. Finally in July
of 1946 under the guidance of Pastor Wm. J. Israel, of Kronthal, the
congregation was organized. Later Pastor Israel asked to be released from his
job and Pastor G. H. Fiechtner of Hazen was asked to carry on.

In August 1946 the trustees purchased Bremen Church, near Hannover and also Lot
15 Block 8, Two blocks from the business district in Beulah, onto which the
Church was set. The Trustees also secured land for a congregation cemetery, and
bought a residence to be used as a parsonage. The new congregation was named
"The Emanuel Congregation of the Evangelical Lutheran Church of the Missouri
Synod", and in addition "The Church of the Lutheran Hour."

The new Church was made ready and was dedicated in March 1947. On June 22 of
that year Pastor Fred Reidel, formerly of Otter Creek was installed as the
first Pastor.

Over the years the Lord was good to this new congregation and soon became
obvious that the congregation had outgrown its House of Worship. Therefore in
1951 its present location was purchased and the Peace Lutheran Church of Hazen
was bought and moved to Beulah and put on a full basement. After adding an
addition, remodeling and the purchasing of new church furniture and organ, the
Church was dedicated to the glory of God on March 9, 1952. The name of the
congregation was then changed to "Concordia Lutheran Church, Missouri Synod,
the Church of the Lutheran Hour."

In April 1953 Rev. Fred Reidel left Beulah to serve a new congregation in
Jamestown. A call was sent to Pastor Samuel Meske of Bancroft, Nebraska. He
accepted and was installed as Pastor on April Ia, 1953. He served faithfully
until December 1954 when he left Beulah to serve a congregation at Wakefield,
Nebraska. During a long period of vacancy Rev. Herbert Burgdor of St. Lukes
Congregation of Zap served as interim Pastor. Finally in March of 1955 it was
decided that Trinity Congregation of Kronthal, and Concordia Congregation of
Beulah, call a Pastor jointly.

In February 1956 Pastor Edwin Rossow, newly graduated from St. Louis Seminary
was Ordained and Installed as the new Pastor. He served until February of 1957
at which time he returned to his home state of Michigan to serve a congregation.

The Congregation then called on Pastor G. H. Feichtner of Hazen to serve as
Interim Pastor and he did so until July of 1957 when Pastor H. O. Carlson of
California was installed and served until February 1962. Again Pastor G. H.
Feichtner of Hazen was summoned to serve the Congregation during its period of
vacancy.

In July 1962 Concordia joined with St. Lukes Congregation of Zap and became a
Parish. Pastor G. H. Feichtner served Concordia Congregation as Interim Pastor
until August 18, 1963 when the present Pastor Melvin Meyer was installed.

Through the years, the Lord has blessed this Congregation. Its number has grown
from six families when the congregation organized to its present membership of
one hundred fifty seven (157) communicant members.

The doors to God's house are open for all who wish to enter. May God grant his
richest blessings on all who walk through its ever open doors.

Our First Pastor

        Rev. and Mrs. Fred Reidel & Frederick

Our Present Pastor

        Rev. and Mrs. Melvin Meyer

CONGREGATIONAL CHURCH

The Congregational Church of Beulah had a fore runner in the Brush Creek
Congregational church, organized in the country south of present Beulah in
1906. This congregation first met in the Hollister home. Later when a school
house was built, services were held in it. The early ministers came from Glen
Ullin to preach to this rural group. Later regular pastors were called. One--
the Rev. Gray--drove from Richardton to serye both Brush Creek and a church
near Fort Clark.

When in 1914 a need was felt by some of the people in the new town for an
English speaking church, a meeting was called and the Rev. Rood from the
Congregational State Office was asked to come to help organize the
Congregational Church of Beulah. At this meeting, held in the Johnson Hall, in
March of 1914 it was voted to list the members of the Brush Creek Congregation
as charter members of the Beulah Church. There were 47 members. They were: Mr.
and Mrs. B. F. Evans and Joseph, Mr. and Mrs. H. J. Roberts, Mr. and Mrs. Geo.
Davidson and Mary, Stephen, Theron and Amber, James Bowdish, Mr. and Mrs. R. E.
Itskins and Dwight, Mrs. W. D. Niehus, Mrs. Wetzel,  Mr. and Mrs. Frank
Schaffer, Mr. and Mrs. Edwin Leese, Fred Leuzinger, Mr. and Mrs. W. C. Kees,
Mrs. Katherine Karlin, Mr. and Mrs. Rudolph Karlin, Walter Remond, Ida Wetzel,
Mr. and Mrs. W. H. Sovereign, Philip Joos, P. I. Sherlock, Mr. and Mrs. H. H.
Kenyon, Mr. and Mrs. E. J. Ehlert. Mr. and Mrs. C. B. Heinemeyer, Frances
Clinger, J. J. Walker, Mr. and Mrs. H. F. Heiple, Mrs. Nettie Tyrrell, Pearl
Sherlock, C. L. Smith and Mrs. A. C. Householder.

The elected officers were: Deacons: B. F. Evans and W. C. Kees; Trustees: J. C.
Evans, Edwin Leese; Treas. Mrs. W. D. Niehus; Clerk, Fred Leuzinger; Organist,
Mrs. Frank Schaffer; Usher, Frank Schaffer; Janitor, Rudolph Karlin.

The records show people of many denominations affiliating with this church in
the early years. Services were held in Johnson Hall until Feb. 1918 when the
services were held in the white school house on the hill north of the Legion
Hall. Then in 1920 the church was built on its present site. Except for one
hired carpenter the labor was all donated by members and other interested
people. The first services were held in the church on Dec. 5, 1920. The need
for securing a bell to sound the call to Worship presented a problem which the
women solved. They made little aprons with big pockets and and sent them "back
east" to relatives and friends with the request for pennies to "ring the Bell."
These contributions plus a generous anonymous donation by a Beulah businessman
made the purchase of the bell possible in time for the New Year's service. The
Ladies Aid was organized in 1917 with 23 members.

The young people organized as a Christian Endeavor group in 1922. This active
group was the predecessor of the present Pilgrim Fellowship.

The Ladies Aid of the church has always been active. In the 1920's ice cream
socials were held on a vacant lot--in the days of Fairs and other celebrations
in town or on the Fairgrounds south of the R. R. tracks, the women always had
food concessions and lemonade stands.

The parsonage was built in 1929. Pastors who have served are: Rev. Mark Wilson,
1917-18; Rev. J. E. Jones, 1918-22; Rev. Noah Garwich, 1922-25; Rev. L. T.
Jones, 1925-26; Rev. H. W. Larsen, 1927; Rev. George Dalzell, 1928; Rev. C. G.
Ellinger, 1929-31; Rev. C. Roemick, 1931-34; Student Pastor, Miss Barbara
Warren, summer 1934; Rev. C. Roemick, 1935-36; Rev. F. J. Wacker, 1936-38;
Student Pastor Russell Milnes, summer 1939; Rev. Norman Davis, 1939-42; Rev.
Orman; Rev. E. Rudolph, 1944; Rev. Huber, 1945; Mrs. Kenneth Fox, spring and
summer 1946; Rev. J. D. Warkentin, 1946-51; Rev. Tyler Kriedeman, 1951-55; Rev.
Ralph Sayler, 1956-63; Rev. Leo Thomas, Sept. & Oct. 1963. The church was
remodeled in 1956-57.

        Top--Left to Right: Mmes. E. Liebelt, R. Heihn, W. Sayler, C.
        Thelander, M. Strom, E. Liebelt, Jr. Bottom--Left to Right: H.
        Schneidt, A. Miller, G. Enderson, J. Kusler, E. Emig.

EVANGELICAL UNITED BRETHEREN CHURCH

The Beulah Evangelical Church was organized in March of 1921, through the
efforts of John and Ludwig Unruh. The previous summer they had made a trip to
Hazen and invited the Rev. H. C. Lehner to conduct services. All that summer
Church and Sunday School services were held at the John Unruh home, but later
that year permission was obtained to hold their services in the school.

In December 1922, it was decided to erect a church building. A lot 50 x 140 ft.
was purchased at a cost of $300.00. The erection of the church building was
begun in June 1923. In spring of that year, the Rev. Lehner was replaced by
Rev. G. H. Bruns. Through the efforts of the Pastor and members, the church was
completed by October 1. The cost of material was $1,087.00. All labor was
donated.

Dedication services were held Oct. 7, 1923. Guest speakers for this occasion
were the Rev. F. H. Brocknueller, Supt. of the Fargo Dist. who dedicated the
church to the services of the Triune God, Rev. Bremmer, Supt. of the Bismarck
District, assisted by Rev. Bruns, Pastor. Three services were held that day.
The morning service theme was "The Struggling Church". The afternoon service was
the dedication service. The evening theme was "The Triumphant Church."

Some of the members at this time were Mr. and Mrs. John Unruh, Mr. and Mrs. L.
Unruh, Nickolus Unruh, Henry Heuther, Mr. and Mrs. Dan Huber, Mr. and Mrs. Dave
Huber, Mr. and Mrs. Adam Bieck, Mr. and Mrs. Emil Nei, Mr. and Mrs. Ed. Mindt,
Mr. and Mrs. John Unruh, Zap, Mr. and Mrs. A. Winkler.

On March 28, 1945 the Church held it's 25th Jubilee. The services started on
Friday evening, with Rev. Lembke as speaker. His sermon was specifically
directed to the young people of the church. Saturday evening speaker was Rev.
Gideon Eberhardt. The main speaker for the Sunday morning and afternoon
services was Rev. Strutz. Rev. Strutz spoke in the German language for the
morning and afternoon services. He ministered in the English language for the
evening services. Rev. Grueneich was pastor of the Beulah Parish at this time.

The following pastors have served this church: Revs. C. Lehner, G. H. Bruns, H.
A. Sayler, K. Himing, R. Bladeau, W. A. Lemke, R. E. Strutz, W. J. Wienands, G.
H. Bruns, C. L. Huber, E. Rudolf, E. Sherbinske, R. Bechde, O. Ketterling, G.
Eberhard and E. Werner.

The Beulah Evangelical Sumday School was organized July 5, 1921 and has held
regular Sunday meetings since that date. The Christian Endeavor was organized
in 1936.

The Beulah E.U.B. has never had a resident Pastor. They have always been served
by the E.U.B. Pastor from Hazen. The Rev. Ed. Werner is the Pastor at the
present time.

The Women's organized work units in the E.U.B. Church is the W.S.W.S.--formerly
called the Ladies Aid. The Friedens E.U.B. Church originated with the merging
of the Strip Mine Church in 1945. The Strip Mine Church was a smaller unit of
the church and was served by the same pastor who served the Hazen and Beulah
Evangelical Churches.

The local W.S.W.S. officers at the present are: President, Mrs. Edwin Werner;
Vice President, Mrs. Harry Wetzel; Secretary, Mrs. Art Tabert; Treasurer, Mrs.
James Thompson.

The work of the W.S.W.S. is to promote the work of the church in its inner
basic activities such as the up-keep and repair of the church building,
sponsoring the Y. F. and Brotherhood through its various secretaries listed
below. Sec. of Spiritual Life, Mrs. Harry Wetzel, Sec. of Christian Social
Relations--Mrs. Otto Reich, Sec. of Christian Education--Mrs. Helen Mindt, Sec.
of Christian Youth--Rev. E. Werner.

The W.S.W.S. is made up of local and national units. Their aim is basically
mission work. They support Missionaries and Missions both at home and abroad.

The W.S.W.S. buys articles needed in the church kitchen and dining room. Other
church activities they sponsor are: congregation dinners, S. S. picnics,
Christmas baskets for the needy, clothes for the needy and we send used eye
glasses, nylons, stamps, and Christmas cards to various organizations to be
used again. "Eyes" is doing wonderful work in this field.

The W.S.W.S. also takes part and prepares programs for the World Service Day
and World Day of Prayer. Above all else it stresses that every woman's work in
the church is important and needed.

THE IMMANUEL BAPTIST CHURCH

The Immanuel Baptist Church of Beulah, was organized in the year, 1910, when
twenty-four people in the Beulah area felt the need for Christian fellowship
with one another. They invited delegates from Hebron, Washburn, and New
Leipzig, N. D. to meet with them and to seek their advice. After much
consideration an agreement was reached and the German Baptist Church was
organized and was known as the Immanuel Church at Evans, N. D. Of the
twenty-four charter members, two are still members. They are Gustav Binder and
Mrs. Amalie Binder.

The Church was moved to Beulah in 1914 and the present parsonage was built the
same year. The present church building was erected in 1930. The parsonage was
also enlarged. The 50th Anniversary of the church was observed in 1960 with a
special celebration.

The following Pastors have served the church through the years: Rev. Frederick
Pahlke, 1910-1912; Rev. Samuel Blumhagen, 1913-1925; Rev. Fred Trautner,
1925-1928, and 1947-1952; Rev. Arthur Fischer, 1935-1937; Rev. Peter Schilling,
1938-1944; Rev. William Jaster, 1915-1947; Rev. Ernest Lautt, 1952-1957; Rev.
John Wood, 1957-1962; and the present Pastor, Rev. Edwin Voigt who came in
Aug., 1962.

The present church membership is eighty. The Sunday School enrollment is eighty
four.

The Women's Missionary Society was organized in 1913 with eight charter
members. One of these, Mrs. Carl Zimbelman, is still an active member. The
present membership is fourteen.

A youth group was also organized. At present it is known as the Commissioned
Baptist Youth (CBY) and has sixteen active members.

THE WOMEN'S MISSIONARY SOCIETY

        Top Row--Left to right: Mrs. A. Ewert, Mrs. Ed. Anderson, Mrs. R.
        Fischer, Mrs. E. Voigt, Mrs. V. Strom. Second Row--Left to right:

EVANGELICAL TRINITY LUTHERAN CONGREGATION-KRONTHAL

Moving a landmark of 60 years standing is more than just moving a building.
With it go ten decades of plans and progress, fond memories and grim
disappointments. This must be so for the rural families in Kronthal as the
Trinity Lutheran church, 14 miles north of Beulah, was moved March 15, 1963 to
Stanton to become the new parish house for Our Savior's Lutheran Church.

The Trinity Evangelical Lutheran church at Kronthal was organized in June,
1898, with the guidance of Rev. P. L. Wohlfeil (now deceased), father of
Leonard Wohlfeil, Hazen, who at that time was pastor of the Hannover
congregation. Those who were active in the founding, as far as could be
ascertained, were: Joseph Boeshans, Fred Wiedenmeyer, Bernard Galster, Peter
Wiedrich, George Miller, Andreas Miller, Johann Koehler, Peter Stern and their
families. Services were held in the homes of the families, which at that time
were sod houses on the homesteads in the new world frontier of the Missouri
river. These pioneers were all Germans who immigrated to this country from
South Russia.

A parish was made up of four congregations: Kronthal, Mannhaven, rural north
Hazen and Wittenberg, north of Zap. Pastors who served during that time were:
Rev. H. Arndt, Rev. J. Duerr, Rev. F. Mathias, Rev. Schmock, Rev. Paul Siegel
and Rev. Elmer Bliss.

In 1903, the congregation purchased three acres of land and built its first
church. Fourteen members were: Friedrich Mettler, Sr., George Miller, Johann
Koehler, Joseph Boeshans, Peter Boeshans, Friedrich Wiedenmeyer, Jacob Hafner,
Michael Eisenbeis, Andres Weidner, Friedrich Pfenning Sr., Christoff Mueller,
August Van der Ohe, Martin Zeiszler, Andreas Miller and Jacob Hafner.

In 1914 the parish was dissolved. Trinity Congregation became self supporting
and decided to build a parsonage and a barn. They purchased another ten acres
of land for pasture for the pastor's horses. They called their first pastor.
Rev. Paul Buck accepted, and served the congregation for seven years from
1914-1921. Members at that time were: Friedrich Mettler Sr., Ferdinand Mettler,
George L. Link, Jacob Dschaak, Andreas Weidner, Joseph Boeshans, Friedrich
Wiedenmeyer, Fred Renner Sr., Henry Reinhardt, Martin Zeiszler, George Miller,
Jacob Christman, Friedrich Pfenning Sr., Johann Koehler Sr., and John Renner Sr.

Rev. Karl Fredericksen succeeded Rev. Buck and served two and a half years. In
1923, the congregation decided to build a new church; 72 ft. long, 32 ft. wide
and with a three quarter size basement to serve as summer school room and a
furnace room. The church had 30--11 ft. pews and 2--10 ft. pews.

        Last Church 1943-1963

In 1924, Rev. P. Schumm accepted a call, but for reasons of ill health, served
only about one year. Rev. Fred Ganske served from 1925-1931; Rev. W. Hennig
from Nov. 1931 to Feb. 1935; Rev. J. H. Schaefer, July 1935 to Aug., 1943. Rev.
Wm. J. Israel, Feb., 1944 to Aug., 1949. Members when the large church was
built included: Friedrich Mettler Sr., Friedrich Wiedenmeyer, Andreas Weidner,
George Miller, Jacob Hafner, Friedrich Pfenning, Sr. Joseph Boeshans, Henry
Reinhardt, Geo. L. Link, Jacob Dschaak, Ferdinand Mettler, John Renner Sr.,
Johann Koehler, John Fischer, Philip Wiedenmeyer, Theo J. Hafner, Mike
Pfenning, John Pfenning, Christian Galster, Fred C. Link, Fred Renner Jr., Fred
Mettler Jr., Joe Reinboldt, Franz Kasper, Jacob Pfenning, John Horning, Albert
Pfenning, Otto Mettler, Joseph Boeshans, Fred Pfenning Jr., Leo Hafner, Ernest
Hafner, Jacob Wiedenmeyer, Christ Zeiszler, August Dschaak, Edward Zeiszler,
Christ Ost, Mrs. C. Fischer, Mrs. C. Miller, Philip Hafner and Daniel Pfenning.

From 1949 to 1956, Trinity had a vacancy and was served by Rev. G. Fiechtner of
Hazen. During that time, they lost a large membership due to the Garrison Dam
and not having a pastor to serve them. In 1955, Concordia Lutheran congregation
of Beulah called a joint meeting and requested of Trinity to call a pastor
jointly, and they agreed.

In 1956, Trinity sold its parsonage and garage. The garage was sold to
Ferdinand Remer. The parsonage was sold to Herb. Tesky who moved it one mile
north and 13 miles east to be remodeled and used as a house on his farm,
(formerly the Leo Bauer farm). It now is the home of Myron Hafner. Rev. E.
Rossow served the two congregations one year and Rev. Hans Carlson served from
Aug., 1957 to Feb., 1962.

        Interior-Kronthal Church

In March, 1962 a joint meeting was called with Concordia Lutheran in Beulah to
call a pastor, but Concordia declined to call jointly. It was Concordia's
wishes to call a minister, which would serve Trinity, but who would not go to
the rural area for summer and confirmation study. Trinity would have to bring
their children to Concordia for instruction.

Trinity received a letter from the Mission Director in June, 1962, stating
there was not a pastor available and that Trinity should close their doors and
bring their membership to the nearest town, Beulah. Some members of Trinity who
had small children felt taking them to Beulah would be a hardship, and
expressed they would join a closer church. This being so, the congregation
could foresee they would be left with still less members, and when the
opportunity arose to sell the church to the Lutheran congregation in Stanton,
it was decided Nov. 23 to sell the church and dissolve.

Voting members were: Ernest R. Hafner, President; Fred P. Boeshans, secretary;
Walter Wiedrich, Treas; Lukas Renner, Elder; Henry Weidner, Elder; Art
Oberlander, Trustee; Ray Morast, Trustee; Ferdinand Renner, Trustee; Eddie
Wiedrich, Herbert Hafner, Harry Hafner, Jacob Weidner and Jacob Renner.

Rev. Bongard from Underwood has served this church the last six months
bi-weekly. The last services were held Feb. 24. This would have been the
church's 65th anniversary.

This church was dedicated at Kronthal Dec. 9, 1923. Forty years-minus one
day--later on Dec. 8, 1963 this church was dedicated as "Our Savior's Lutheran
Church", in Stanton, N. D.

Kronthal Cemetery Corporation was organized in April, 1963 for the purpose of
maintaining the cemetery at the former church site. A monument will be erected
there, and the inscription will be: "In memory of the Christian Pioneers of
Evangelical Lutheran Congregation, Kronthal. Organized--1898. First
Church--1903-1923. Last Church--1943-1963."

THE NEU GLEUCKSTAHL CONGREGATIONAL CHURCH

        1945--To present time

This Congregational Church (or gemeinde) was organized on November 15, 1905.
The organizational meeting was called to order by Dr. Ewertz, from Chicago,
Illinois and the Rev. J. F. Reister. This meeting was held at the Peter Kessler
Sr. home. They chose the name Neu Gleuckstahl, because many of the members had
originally lived in that little Russian "dorf".

There were sixteen Charter members: Mr. and Mrs. Peter Kessler Sr., Mr. and
Mrs. Jacob Kessler, Mr. and Mrs. Peter Kessler Jr., Mr. and Mrs. Philip Flemmer
Sr., Mr. and Mrs. Johann Schilling, Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Schilling, Mr. and Mrs.
Johann Retzer, Mr. and Mrs. Johann Weltz, Mr. and Mrs. Nickalous Unruh, Mr. and
Mrs. Jacob Unruh, Mr. and Mrs. Frederick Walth, Mr. and Mrs. Johann Flemmer,
Mr. and Mrs. Jacob P. Flemmer; Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Huber and Mr. Michael Voegele
Sr. There are none of the Charter Members living.

The worship services and prayer meetings were held regularly, children were
baptized, marriages performed, all at the Kessler home. The people would come
by horses, wagons, buggies, stone-boat or on foot. It was nothing unusual to
see a family walk "cross country" to church on Sunday. The services continued
to be held at the Kessler home until 1910.

The first couple married in the Neu Gleucksthal Church were Mr. and Mrs. John
Daffee. The first baby baptized was Bertha Flemmer (Mrs. Reynold Kessler) the
daughter of Mr. and Mrs. J. P. Flemmer.

The first death was Peter Kessler Sr.

After many struggles and overcoming enumerable hardships the Neu Gleucksthal
Congregation built their first House of Worship. All of the lumber had to be
hauled by horse and wagon from Glen Ullin. This little white frame church
served the congregation until 1945. The members of Neu Gleucksthal began to
realize the need for a new and larger church as early as in the 1940's, but the
building actually didn't get started until 1945. The old church was torn down
and the present structure was erected. The Pastor serving the congregation
during this building program was Rev. John Hoersch. In December, 1945, the Neu
Gleucksthal Church was completed and was duly dedicated with a special
Dedication Service.

Neu Gleucksthal is proud of the two sons of the congregation who have entered
the ministry.

Pastor John Flemmer (deceased). He was the son of Philip and Katherine Flemmer.
During his years of faithful laboring in the ministry of the church, he served
six churches in the State of Colorado, one in Washington and one in Nebraska.
He died in Colorado on January 15, 1962.

Pastor Jacob Kessler, is the son of the late Jacob and Katharina Kessler. He
served various Congregational Churches in Idaho and is now retired from the
ministry and lives in Walla Walla, Washington.

The following names are listed on the 1964 records of the church as their
present members. Mrs. Anna Kessler, Mr. and Mrs. Fred K. Voegele, Mr. and Mrs.
Fred D. Voegele, Mr. and Mrs. Ed. D. Voegele, Mr. and Mrs. Ferdinand Voegele,
Mr. and Mrs. Alvin Flemmer, Mr. and Mrs. Wilbert Flemmer, Mr. and Mrs. Alvin
Moos, Mr. and Mrs. Henry Redman, Mr. and Mrs. Fred Huber Jr.

Over a period of fifty-nine years the congregation has been served by
twenty-two pastors--Pastor Veith is the present Pastor.

There have been 122 children baptized, 106 confirmed, 46 marriages were
performed, and 45 deaths.

PEACE LUTHERAN CHURCH

        The First Peace Lutheran in 1907

On December 30, 1906 the Peace Lutheran Church south of Beulah was organized;
thus this congregation is one of the oldest in Mercer County. The following are
the charter members who signed the constitution: Jacob Flemmer, Christian
Flemmer, Adam Fuchs, Philip Hausauer, Johann Jakober, George Inrig, Christian
Lapp, George Schnaidt, Daniel and Michael Voegele. The first deacons of the
newly organized congregation were J. Flemmer, C. Lapp, and G. Schnaidt.  Rev.
William Klein of Rosebud was the first pastor to serve the congregation. Mr. C.
Lapp and Mr. Schnaidt donated land for the church and cemetery. The first
church was dedicated in July 1907. Rev. Huth served the congregation from 1908
to 1910 and Rev. H. Pirner from 1910 to 1913. In 1912 a parsonage was built,
which was sold the following year. The next pastor of the Congregation was
Pastor J. Mau; he served until 1915. In 1914 the Peace Lutheran church and the
newly organized Zion Lutheran Church in Beulah became a parish. Rev. A.
Lieberodt was the next pastor, he served from 1916-1919; he was succeeded by
Rev. M. Baudler who served the congregation for 4 1/2 years until 1923. Then
Rev. W. Siefkes accepted a call to the parish which he served until 1926. Rev.
S. Joachim of Beulah was the next pastor of Peace church, which he served until
1928. In this year Zion Lutheran of Beulah became independent, and the Peace
church formed a parish with the Emanuel Lutheran church of Zap; thus the next
pastor was Rev. E. Fuchs who served the Peace church longer than any other
pastor, namely for 11 years until 1939.

        The 2nd Peace Lutheran Church destroyed in 1941

In 1932 (July 3) it was decided to buy the Reformed church in Beulah which was
dedicated Oct. 6, 1932. After Rev. Fuchs left, Rev. Huetmeyer was called by the
parish. On June 18, 1941 the old church was totally destroyed by a tornado, but
soon it was decided to build a new church. This was begun in January 1942 and
on May 3, 1942 the new and present church was dedicated.

Rev. Huetmeyer retired from the ministry in August 1946 and the parish was
vacant at this time, Rev. H. Dirks of Beulah served as supply pastor.

On December 6, 1946, the Peace church observed the 40th anniversary in 2
services conducted by the pastors R. Heupel and H. Dirks.

The congregation is now 58 years old and is said to be one of the oldest
congregations in Mercer County. During these years the official acts that were
performed were: 330 baptisms, 317 confirmed, 77 couples joined their hands in
holy matrimony, 101 funeral services conducted and 3408 knelt at the altar to
receive Holy Communion.

The present officers serving are: Fred Entze, deacon; John Gunsch, Trustee;
Louie Ballensky, Secretary and Emil Frank, Treasurer.

The divine services are being held by Rev. J. Schmierer as supply pastor.

May the Lord of the church continue to bless this church and its members with
all spiritual blessings to the praise and glory of His name and to the
salvation of many immortal souls.

This Peace Church, Lord, we now command
Unto Thy care, and pray: Defend
And shield it from calamity,
Dwell there now and eternally.

        Present Peace Lutheran Church

ST. BENEDICT CATHOLIC CHURCH

It was in 1904 when the first priest, Father Alois Strigl, came to the St.
Benedict community to offer Mass and organize a Catholic parish. During 1904
and 1905 Mass was offered at the Martin Schutt farm home. ln 1906 Mass was
offered at the Wendelin Zahn farm home, by Father Benedict Peter.

In 1906 plans for the erection of a church became a reality. Construction began
that same year but the church was not completed until 1907. The cost of
materials for this church was $2,000. The labor was donated by members of the
parish. A six acre tract of land was donated for the church site, which is
located about 14 miles southeast of Beulah. The St. Benedict church was
Consecrated by Bishop Wherle of the Bismarck diocese.

Priests from the Assumption Abbey in Richardton, who served the parish after
Fr. Benedict Peter, in their respective order were: Father Benedict Seethaler,
Father Maurice Hilpert, Father George Kein, Father Thomas Starkle and Father
Ambrose Johanns. The first choir was organized by Father Benedict Peter in 1908.

In 1925 an addition was built to the church at a cost of $1,800.00. It was
enlarged again in 1926, cost of which was $1,200.00. In 1930 a parish house was
constructed at a cost of $7,000.00. All these building projects were
accomplished without any expenditure for labor, as it was all donated.

After the completion of the parish house in 1930, Father Nickolis Fox became
the first resident pastor of St. Benedict's parish, and served until 1933. He
was replaced by Father Joseph Meiser who served until his death in 1940. Father
Meiser is the only priest buried in the St. Benedict cemetery. Father Conrad
Lotter served through 1941. Father Bernhardt Eichner succeeded him. Father
Eichner still offers daily Mass and furnishes the spiritual needs to the
twenty-seven families in the parish, despite his eighty-five years of age.

The first Baptism in this new church was administered by Father Peter on June
15, 1908 to Joseph Deibert. The first, First Holy Communion class was received
in 1910, and the first class was confirmed in 1917.

The maximum number of families belonging to St. Benedict's parish numbered
about 45. They sponsored various activities, such as bazaars and dinners in the
1920's, 30's and 40's to help finance the needs of the parish.

The St. Benedict parish also has an inspiring record of religious vocations,
with one Brother, Martin Heier; seven nuns: Magdeline Heier, Sister Sylvia;
Bardella Weiand, Sister Coletta; Claudia Weiand, Sister Virginia; Ann Deibert,
Sister Mary Margaret; Delores Skalsky, Sister Lambert; Margaret Eberle, Sister
Martha and Alice Weiand, Sister Sylveria.

With the church receiving the constant attention of its parishioners, it offers
proof of being maintained in a becoming manner, with a new Tabernacle and a
recent paint job. It is viewed as a gem of the one time western prairie, where
Almighty God lent a helping hand to those who needed Him.

KNIGHTS OF COLUMBUS

The local Knights of Columbus had its beginning in June of 1955 with 22 men of
the St. Joseph Parish in Beulah forming a club as an affiliate of the Bishop
Ryan Council of Glen Ullin. At its organizational meeting the following
officers were elected: A. W. Mosbrucker, President; George Koppi,
Vice-President; and Mike Held, Secretary-Treasurer. This group operated as such
until January of 1959 when a full-fledged Knights of Columbus Council, known as
Pope Pius XII Council was instituted. The Most Reverend Hillary B. Hacker,
Bishop of Bismarck, along with many other local and state Knights of Columbus
officials, was present for the installation banquet. The present Knights of
Columbus Council includes approximately 65 members representing St. Joseph's
Parish of Beulah, St. Martin's Parish of Hazen, and St. Benedict's Parish of
rural Beulah.

The Knights of Columbus is an organization of Catholic men. A few of the more
worth-while projects carried out by the local Council are: an annual fund
raising campaign, the proceeds of which go toward the support of the Home on
the Range for Boys; an annual Christmas basket program whereby foodstuffs,
clothing, and toys are distributed to needy families in the local Council area;
and participation in the State 6-Point Program, a program designed to help
local Councils compete on a statewide basis for the Outstanding Council Award
presented annually.

Pope Pius XII Council of Beulah would like to take this opportunity to extend
heartiest congratulations to Beulah and surrounding territory upon the
celebration of its Golden Jubilee. May the ensuing 50 years be as fruitful as
the first 50.

SAINT JOSEPH'S CATHOLIC CHURCH

        1915-1938

The beginning and growth of the Catholic Church in the area is intimately
associated with the development of Beulah. The immigration of various people,
settlement of the land and the coming of the railroad are all part of the
story. Catholics were in the area long before Beulah was established; dating
back to the French fur traders and trappers, and intermarriage with the
Indians. The shortage of priests made the development of the church slow.
Travel was another barrier. This imposed much concern to the early settlers in
their endeavor to fulfill religious obligations.

During the horse and buggy days there were no priests in Beulah; although the
Benedictine Fathers from Bismarck and the Abbey at Richardton served the
Halliday mission. Much credit must be given to these energetic and zealous
Fathers. Beulah Catholics were parishioners of the Halliday mission and all
records were maintained there. After the coming of the railroad, early settlers
recall attending Mass in the upstairs of the Rose Hardware.

        1938

The first priest in the vicinity was Rev. Bonaventure Goebel O. S. B., June 1,
1914 to Mar. 1916, with residency at St. Alexius Hospital, Bismarck, N. D. Mass
was seldom celebrated more than once a month. This was a busy occasion for the
priest. Besides instructing the children he conferred the sacraments to those
under his jurisdiction. Early priests were: Rev. E. Bubic O. S. B. Mar. 21,
1916 to Mar. 1917; Rev. B. Goebel Mar. 1917 to 1919; Rev. F. X. Braining, July
1916 to Oct. 1921, all associated with the Abbey at Richardton.

Typical of his usual eagerness for the promotional growth of Beulah, P. S.
Chaffee in 1915 donated $500.00 and a tract of land (still intact except for
that portion taken over by the city of Beulah to serve as a street) to
establish a Catholic Church. An additional grant by the Catholic Extension
Society and substantial donations from parishioners, saw the completion of a
small frame church in 1915. This church was dedicated to St. Joseph. It served
until 1938 when it was dismantled and the present building was erected. Money
for this church came from another Extension Society grant, assessments, and
donations of money and labor by the members of the parish.

The first Baptism was June 1, 1916, Mary Eva Ussleman, parents: John Usselman
and Margaret Giesinger, administered by Father Bubic. First marriage: Frederick
Almendinger and Katherine Bosch Jan. 12, 1919. First burial: Kathlene Evans,
age 24, 1918.

        The St. Anne Altar Society

The need for priests in the Bismarck diocese became so acute that Bishop Wherle
in 1920 was prompted to appeal to the German speaking Fathers of the Precious
Blood Community, Carthegena, Ohio to take over several parishes. Over the years
the Precious Blood Fathers have assumed the responsibility of supplying priests
in this area. These Fathers are remembered with fondness and respect: Rev. E.
J. Olberding, CPPS, the first of these priests, served from Oct. 1921 to Aug.
1927; Charles A. Bauer CPPS, from Sept. 1927 to Sept. 1937; Herman Schweitzer
CPPS, Sept. 1937 to Sept. 1941; H. Wibbels CPPS, Sept. 1941 to Aug. 1948.
However, St. Joseph's Church was still missionary in character, as these
priests resided at Hazen.

Father Ray Guillozet CPPS was assigned to the parish in 1948 as the first
resident priest. The Rectory was built that year. Much of the work was done by
this ambitious and talented priest. Evidence of his skill is the church altar
and numerous other pieces of handcraft. Father Rupert Landoll CPPS assumed the
duties of parish priest from Sept. 1955 to Aug. 1963. The church gained much
from his knowledge and experiences. He had ambitions for the youth of the
church. He promoted a choir of high school students and endeavored to establish
religious educational facilities. He was especially eager for the development
of an industry in Beulah, spending much time and extensive travel towards that
aim. Father A. Jungwirth CPPS came to Beulah in Sept. 1963. Although new to the
community he quickly made the acquaintance of his parish members and they are
very appreciative of his wisdom and congeniality.

St. Joseph's Church has grown with the community and an integral part of it
spiritually; are in harmony with its civic growth and development and in their
hearts have put their trust in God for Beulah's future.

        Father Ray Guillozet--1st Parisb Priest

        Father Rupert Landoll--1955-1963

ZION LUTHERAN CHURCH HISTORY

        This church and parsonage was located on the same location as the
        present church and parsonage

Lutheran people settled in the area known as Farrington even before the
beginning of the town of Beulah. With the building of the railroad, towns came
into existence along this new road and Beulah also had its beginning. Zion
Lutheran Church was organized in the same year.

The people who lived in the area before the beginning of Zion Lutheran Church
were served by Pastor A. Schult of Stanton, North Dakota. It was on January 10,
1914 that Pastor J. Mau from Rhein, North Dakota came to Beulah to conduct the
service and to meet with the congregation after the service regarding the
establishment of a congregation herein this community. After the service
discussions were held which led to organization. Pastor Mau was then asked to
serve the congregation at Beulah along with his other duties in the parishes of
Nisbet, Rhein, and Peace Congregation, (South of Beulah). He accepted this, but
later Beulah, Peace Congregation separated from the other parishes and Pastor
Mau was asked to serve these two parishes. A parsonage was built and dedicated
on August 30, 1914, and just a few days after the dedication the people began
building a church. The building of the church was completed in two months and
the new church building was dedicated on November 15, 1914. Pastor Mau resigned
on October 10, 1915 having led the congregation during the trying days of its
beginnings. The charter members according to the records were: Jacob Mauch,
Karl Hilz Sr., Ludwig Sailer, Edward Kerner, Hemrich Becker, George Schnaidt,
Emmanuel Mattheis, John Sailer, Adolf Schuh, Philip Becker, Emmanuel Heth, Fred
Reub, Fred M. Reub, John Fischer, and F. Niehus. After the resignation of
Pastor Mau in October of 1915, the congregation was vacant until February of
1916 when Pastor A. Lieberodt from Heil, North Dakota accepted the call to
become shepherd of Zion Lutheran. It wasn't long until this pastor was made to
feel at home here in Beulah. A number of improvements were made during his
pastorate. World War I and the Flu brought the congregation trials. A number of
the sons of the congregation served in the war and some were stricken with the
flu. Pastor Lieberodt also contracted the disease and finally had to retire
because of his illness. The congregation showed its appreciation for his
service by paying for the hospital expenses as well as his salary.

Again the congregation became vacant and now for a longer period of time.
During this vacancy the congregation turned to Pastor Schmidt of Zap, North
Dakota for service. Pastor M. Baudler arrived from Texas in March of 1919.
During his pastorate the congregation continued to grow and numbered at one
time fifty families. The last debt on the church was paid. Pastor Baudler
served Zion Lutheran until June 1923.

In January, 1924 Pastor W. J. Siefkes led the congregation for the first time
at its annual meeting. Under his quiet and able leadership the congregation
matured and developed. Pastor Siefkes too, had to resign because of illness in
August of 1926.

The congregation then called Pastor S. Joachim who arrived in Beulah in October
of 1926. He was to continue serving the congregation until his death on January
1, 1943. During his pastorate the congregation grew in spite of many struggles
and hardships. These were the drought years, depression, language questions,
and building. Yet the congregation grew. During his time also the parish was
divided enabling Pastor Joachim to give his full time to Zion Lutheran. The
parsonage was modernized as much as possible. Because of the growth of the
congregation more facilities were needed. A new church was built and dedicated
in November of 1940. This church is still in use today.

Pastor Henry Dirks was called to Zion Lutheran and served the congregation from
October of 1943 to April of 1949. Many of the vexing problems were overcome
step by step. Double services were started and the language problem became a
side issue.

During the vacancy from April 1949 to November of 1949 the congregation
installed a new heating system in the church and also decided to build a new
parsonage. Pastor John Kammerer arrived in Beulah on November 1, 1949 and
served the congregation faithfully until August of 1961 when he accepted a call
to Ashley, North Dakota where he is serving today. The congregation grew
rapidly under his able leadership and many improvements were made.

        PRESENT CHURCH

New property was bought, (the Muhlhauser) and is still used today for Sunday
School rooms. Still it was not long until, the congregation realized that
something needed to be done concerning buildings, in order to have more room
for worship and education. The congregation began talking about this problem
already in 1956 and a building fund was started. In 1962 plans were proposed to
the congregation which were adopted to build a new church and education unit on
new property. The church is now under construction. Soon after the congregation
purchased the land north of the Beulah Community Nursing home, the Beulah
Public School purchased the property to the west of this new location and both
buildings are now steadily taking shape.

During the vacancy when Pastor Kammerer left for Ashley, the congregation again
turned to its neighbors for help. Pastor Buhs of Zap, North Dakota and Pastor
Schmierer, Chaplain of Beulah Community Nursing Home and Joachim Memorial Home
served the congregation until January of 1962 Pastor M. Schumacher arrived in
January of 1962 and was installed on January 7. Zion Lutheran Church has had a
steady growth to the present membership of 921 baptized and 667 confirmed
members. At the beginning of the year 1964 the Sunday School had an enrollment
of 253. Under God's hand and the able leadership of His servants it has
weathered many storms and is today also celebrating its own 50th Anniversary of
organization. We look back with grateful hearts to God who has given His word
and also the fruit of that word.

During the fifty years of history the congregation has grown with the community
and kept pace with the times. Under God's help we strive to continue to glorify
His name and praise Him in Zion.
