Office at Killdeer, North Dakota. Previous to this, she was employed at
Colorado Springs, Colorado, and at Glendive, Montana. Other dividends are five
grandchildren, namely, Zola Kay and Renae Kostelnak; Marlowe F. and Carma
Cleone Kulish; and Marty Carlo Tatum.

As a young lad August enjoyed the sport of baseball. Many of Mercer County's
older residents will remember the games he was involved in, that were played at
Krem, Expansion, Mannhaven, Kronthal, and later at Hazen and Beulah. He
actively participated in the sport until about 1957. He still participates as
umpire and is an avid spectator. In recent years bowling has become his
favorite game. Mr. Miller is a member of Concordia Lutheran Church of Beulah,
North Dakota.

Mrs. Miller gives of her time to the Women's Fellowship, which is an
organization for all women who are members and interested friends of the
Congregational Church. She serves her community by being a member of the Red
Cross Volunteer Grey Ladies. She is a familiar and a welcome person to many of
the residents who make their home at Joachim's Memorial Home at Beulah, one of
which is her mother, Mrs. Katherine Neher. Mrs. Miller is a member of the
Congregational Church at Beulah, North Dakota.

GOTTFRIED AND JULIA RAESZLER

Gottfried Raeszler was born on August 23, 1889 in S. Russia to Jacob and
Rosina, nee Heine, Raeszler. The Raeszler's father died when the children were
still quite young. Rosina thought that there would be many more opportunities
for her boys to make a good living in America. She had heard of how they were
giving land free to anyone who was over twenty-one and willing to farm it. In
1904 Rosina and her four boys; Gottfried, Adolph, John and David immigrated to
the United States. They took up a homestead southeast of Beulah. Gottfried
lived here with his mother and brothers until he was twenty-one. He then filed
claim on a piece of property north of Halliday.

Julia Raeszler was born July 16, 1894 in Leibzig, Russia, a daughter of Michael
and Christina, nee Krueger, Giese. In 1900 she immigrated to the United States
with her parents and younger sister, Christina, now Mrs. Geo. Vollmer of
Killdeer. The Gieses lived south of Hebron for three years and then moved to a
farm near Bowdish.

Julia and Gottfried were married on June 29, 1913, at the Ronda Reformed
Church, by Pastor J. G. Fertsch. They lived on Gottfried's homestead until
1916, when they moved to a farm about six miles north of Beulah. Here the
Raeszlers farmed and raised their famay. They retired in 1947 and moved into
Beulah. Gottfried passed away on May 16, 1957. Julia still lives in Beulah,
where she is a member of the Zion Lutheran Church.

The union of Julia and Gottfried Raeszler was blessed with nine children; Leo
has two sons, Aarron and Harlen, by his first wife, Ida Tabert (deceased). He
is now married to the former Esther Leu and lives in Malvern, Iowa. Ervin is
single and lives with his mother. Reinhold married Renata Wallender and has two
children, Norma and Ronald. They live on a farm north of Beulah. Jacob died at
the age of four. Rubin of Carlsbad, California, married Tillie Beck They have
six sons; Rudolph, Larry, Melvin, Jimmie, Alvin, and Darrold. Hilda married
Sandy Schulz. They live at Bismarck and have three children; Connie, Bryan, and
Kevin. Linda married Eugene Voegele. They have three sons; Virgil, Randy, and
Bruce, and live on a farm north of Beulah. Richard married Mrs. Josephine
Stumpf and has five stepchildren; John, LeRoy, Linda, Ronald and Dale. They
live in Seattle, Wash. Henry married Darlene Huber and lives at Bismarck, N.
Dak They have two children Scott and Dana Ann. There are eighteen grandchildren
and five step-grandchildren.

        Ronald, Reine, Renata and Norma

REINHOLD AND RENATA RAESZLER

Reinhold Gottfried Raeszler was born on September 8, 1919, on his parents' farm
six miles northwest of Beulah. His parents were Gottfried and Julia Raeszler.
He has spent his entire life in Mercer County, where he received his education
in the rural schools. He farmed with his parents until his marriage to the
former Renata Wallender.

Renata was born on November 1, 1918 on a farm in the Otter Creek vicmity,in
Oliver county. Her parents are Fred and Ida, nee Schwagler, Wallender. She grew
to womanhood on her parents farm and received her education in the rural Oliver
County schools. She moved to Beulah with her parents in 1939 and worked as a
clerk in Mike Held's Grocery Store until her marriage to Reine.

Renata and Reine were married on October 19, 1941 at Zion Lutheran Church, Rev.
S. Joachim officiated. They bought the former Charley Niedermeyer farm, 4 1/2
miles northwest of Beulah, which they are farming at the present time. Even
though they started farming in comparatively recent times--they remember that
when they started they did some farming with horses and for quite a few years
they had no electricity on their farm. In 1948 they built a new modern home.
They farm on a diversified basis 475 acres in grain farming, run about 50 head
of range cattle and they have 5 dairy cows.

They were blessed with two children; Norma and Ronald. Norma Arlene was born
March 24, 1944 at Beulah. She is a Sophomore at the Jamestown College where she
is majoring in nursing.

Ronald Reinhold was born March 29, 1949 at Beulah. He is a Freshman at the
Beulah High School, where he is active in F.F.A., band, chorus, and the Science
Club. He is the accompanist for the Junior Choir and Sunday School at the Zion
Lutheran Church. He is also a member of the Spring Creek 4-H club.

The Raeszlers are members of the Zion Lutheran Church. Renata is a member of
the Women of the Church Organization. Reinhold has been a member of the Church
Council and Building Committee. Renata is also a member of the Northern Star
Homemakers. Reinhold is an assistant 4-H leader and a member of the Central
Bowling League.

MR. AND MRS. JOHN REINHARDT

John Henry Reinhardt was born on a farm north of Beulah on November 4, 1904,
the son of Henry and Magdalena (Koehler) Reinhardt. He attended Kronthal School
until the age of 14, at which time he furthered his education at Concordia
College in St. Paul. On January 22, 1929 he married Marie Lapp.

Marie was born May 29, 1907 in South Russia, the daughter of Martin and
Fredericka (Huber) Lapp. She came to the United States with her parents in
1908. They settled south of Beulah where she attended school and lived until
she married.

The Reinhardt's first home, after their marriage was a farm 23 miles north of
Hazen, here they farmed until March of 1934. They sold their farm and purchased
the home they still occupy in Beulah.

John started working at the Farmers Union Elevator in 1939 as secondman under
Edward Vasichek. In 1947 Mr. Vasichek was transferred to Bismarck and John took
over the managership, a job he still holds.

The John Reinhardts take an active part in the activities of the Concordia
Lutheran Church of which they are Charter Members. For the last four years
Marie has been Chairman of the Mercer County Heart Fund.

The Reinhardts were blessed with five children, three girls and two boys.
Archie died as an infant, Donna and Marvin died in 1953 and 1956. Corrine and
Margie are married and have given John and Marie four grandchildren.


        Corrine             Marvin

Corrine Reinhardt Walz

Corrine was born on June 19, 1930 at Beulah. She attended school in Beulah,
graduating from high schoolin 1948. She attended college in Seward, Nebraska
and Dickmson State Teachers College, where she received her Bachelor of Science
Degree in Elementary Education.

On August 13, 1950, Corrine married Leon Walz, son of Mr. and Mrs. Herman Walz
of rural Beulah. The children born to this marriage are three daughters;
Pamela, age 12; Pernell age 10, and Gail age 4.

Since their marriage their teaching careers have taken them to many communities
such as; Pick City, Emmet, Riverdale, Zap, Dickinson and Rocky Boy, Montana,
where they are teaching at the present time.

Marvin Reinhardt

Marvin was born March 2, 1932 at Beulah. He attended school in Beulah, where he
graduated in 1951. He then attended college in Greeley, Colorado and Moorhead,
Minnesota. He spent 3 years in the United States Air Force. Upon his discharge
he entered Concordia Theological Seminary in Springfield, Illinois, which he
attended for two years. He returned home to Beulah to find summer employment.
Marvin died in a construction accident on July 18, 1956 in Hazen. He had
reached the age of 24.

        Donna             Margie

Donna Reinhardt

Donna was born March 15, 1935 in Beulah. She attended school in Beulah
graduating in 1953. Donna was employed at Bismarck Grocery Wholesale Company at
the time of her death. She was killed in a car accident on October 30, 1953 at
the age of 18.

Margie Reinhardt Stiefel

Marchie (Margie) was born January 1, 1938. She attended school in Beulah and
graduated in 1955. She attended Humboldt Institute of Minneapolis. Upon her
graduation she was employed in Beulah and later in Minneapolis. She then began
working for Bell Telephone Company in Bismarck in 1958. She was transferred to
Fargo to a management position for the company in 1960, a job she held until
her marriage on August 11, 1961, to Ivan Stiefel, son of Mr. and Mrs. John
Stiefel of Stanton. Their marriage has been blessed with one son Jeffrey, who
was born in June of 1962. They now reside in Stanton. Mr. Stiefel is Mercer
County Sheriff.

FREDERICK AND CHARLOTTA REUB, SR.

        Grandma Reub

Frederick Reub Sr. was born at Hoffnungstahl, S. Russia on August 4, 1855 to
Philip and Elizabeth, nee Kirschman, Reub.

Charlotta Reub was born in Peterstahl, S. Russia on Feb. 3, 1860, to Franz and
Charlotta, nee Broeckel, Schopp.

The Reub's were married on January 10, 1883. To this union were born 5
children; Frederick (deceased), Elizabeth, Mrs. Philip Werner, Adam, Katharina,
Mrs. John Krause, and John (deceased).

During the early years of their marriage, Charlotta began to help her
mother-in-law, who was a mid-wife. Charlotta seemed to have a natural talent
for this sort of thing, so the Gov't Doctor, Dr. Felcher (who came to the
village once a month began to teach her some of the fundamentals of medicine.
Soon she was setting bones, delivering babies and administering to the sick.
After she had studied and worked with the Dr. for the required length of time,
she received a Certificate of Registration issued by the Russian Government.

In 1905 the Reub's immigrated to America. The first year they lived in Velva.
They came to Mercer County in 1906 and took up a homestead southeast of Beulah.
The Reub's were Charter Members of the Zion Lutheran Church of Beulah.

Frederick passed away on April 17, 1922. Charlotta farmed for one more year and
then moved into Beulah. Here she soon became a well-known figure amongst the
immigrant homesteaders. She had also come from Russia and spoke the same
language, so most of them had more confidence in "Grandma Reub" than in some of
the local doctors. She never refused to "attend" anyone who came to her door.
Though she spoke only German, language was no barrier, whether it was an
"Englisher" or one of the Indians, who lived north of Beulah, she somehow would
understand their needs and they would understand her. One could almost call her
the "lady with the lamp", for by her little kerosene lamp, setting by the
kitchen window, one would know where she was. If it burned bright, she was
home; if it was turned low, she was in the neighborhood; if it was out, she was
out on a call. It is a conservative estimate that during this time she
delivered more than a thousand babies. She had a will of iron and was afraid of
nothing. She never locked a door, so if anyone needed her you could always go
into her house and lie on the cot in her porch until she came back. She went
whenever she was called and in all kinds of weather. She was no stranger to our
North Dakota blizzards.

In later years she made three trips to Calif. by train. She still had not
learned the English language, but that did not stop her, and she had no trouble
getting there. But when she returned and spoke of San France-Angeles, one never
knew which of the two California cities had been honored by a visit from this
little immigrant midwife from North Dakota.

She died on August 27, 1952, having reached the age of 92.

FREDERICK REUB JR. HISTORY

Frederick Reub Jr. was born in Alt Hofnungstahl S. Russia. He was the oldest of
the Reub children. He received his education in the schools in Russia. He was a
young man of 20 when his parents decided to immigrate to America.

In 1906 Fred filed claim on 160 acres east of Beulah. He broke the land with a
walking plow and a team of horses.

In 1911 he married Elizabeth Schlect. She had come over from Russia with her
step-brother John Krauz, in 1910.

Fred and Lisa were married on October 1, 1911 by Rev. Schult, a Lutheran
Pastor. There was no organized Lutheran Congregation; so they were married in a
nearby school.

The couple lived on Fred Jr.'s homestead until 1939. All of the children were
born here and grew to adulthood on this farm.

When they needed steel for the East Bridge (near Beulah) Fred Jr. was one of
the men who went by wagon to New Salem to haul the steel. So that the long trip
over wasn't made with an empty wagon he'd haul over a load of wheat. Fred also
dug many wells in and around Beulah. Many of these are still in existence today.

In 1939 the Reubs moved into town, altho Fred continued to farm with the help
of his sons until 1952. He sold 240 acres to Knife River Coal Co., gave Herbert
the home place and Jake received another quarter. Jake lives on the Frederick
Sr. and Charlotta Reub farm.

The Reubs were charter members of Zion Lutheran Church, of which they remained
members.

Even after retiring Fred remained active and liked to putter. Every house he
lived in he would improve and remodel. The last house the Reubs moved in and
remodeled was Grandma Reub's.

Fred suffered a stroke from which he never fully recovered. After a long
illness Fred passed away in April 14, 1961. Since Fred's death Lisa has
remained in their house in Beulah. The last year, she has been quite ill and
much of her time has been spent in the hospital.

The Reub Children are: Clara, Mrs. Bill Werner of Center; Jake of Beulah
(married to Pauline Heihn); Herb of Beulah, (married to Ida Weil); Erna, Mrs.
Howard Johnson of Underwood; Elsie, Mrs. Gordon Goetz of Hazen. There are
sixteen grandchildren and sixteen great grandchildren. This page sponsored by
Mrs. Elizabeth Reub and family.

MR. AND MRS. WILHELM HETH

Wilhelm Heth son of Michael and Louise (nee Mackus) Heth was born May 29, 1885
in Bessarabia, South Russia. On Dec. 6, 1906 he married Lydia Kruger daughter of
Carl and Katherina (nee Mauch) Kruger. She was born Oct. 16, 1885 in Bessarabia,
South Russia.

Mr. and Mrs. Heth came to America in the late summer of 1909, and lived with
Mr. Heth's Uncle Gottlob and Aunt Christina (nee Heth) Schuh, in the Expansion
area north of Hazen.

They homesteaded south of Golden Valley, North Dakota from 1911 until 1916.
They came to the Beulah area and farmed till they moved into Beulah in 1947.

To this union were born nine children, two of which died, and the remaining are
Anna (Mrs. Rudolph Schnaidt) Beulah; Henry of Fargo, North Dakota; Art of
Moorhead, Minnesota; Bill of Beulah, North Dakota; Fred of Lester Prairie,
Minn.; Pauline (Mrs. Ervin Mettler), of Bismarck, North Dakota; and Otto of
Dillon, Montana.

Mr. Heth died July 30, 1949, and he was a faithful member of Zion Lutheran
Church. Mrs. Heth resides in Beulah and is still a faithful member of Zion
Lutheran Church.

MR. AND MRS. GEORGE SCHNAIDT

George Schnaidt son of Christian and Katherina (nee Schutz) Schnaidt was born
Sept. 27, 1875 in Gleuckstahl, South Russia. On Nov. 27, 1901 he married
Karolina Burkhardt, daughter of Rudolph and Barbara (nee Maier) Burkhardt. She
was born April 20, 1879 in Kassel, South Russia.

In the fall of 1905 Mr. and Mrs. Schnaidt and one daughter Rosina came to this
country. They stayed one winter with their Uncle and Aunt--The Michel Keim's of
Eureka, South Dakota.

In 1906 they came to Mercer County and homesteaded about 20 miles south of
Beulah. In the fall of 1911 they moved 2 miles north of Beulah.

To this union were born thirteen children, five of which are deceased. The
remaining are: Rosina (Mrs. John Raszler) Ryder, North Dakota; Rudolph, Carl,
Emil of Beulah, North Dakota; Rev. G. V. Schnaidt, Billings, Montana; Reinhold
and Adam of Calif.; Martha (Mrs. Henry Flessner), Milwaukee, Wisconsin.

Mr. Schnaidt died Dec. 4, 1924; Mrs. Schnaidt retired and moved to Beulah, in
1940. She died August 5, 1950.

Mr. Schnaidt was a charter member of Zion Lutheran Church and both were
faithful church members.

MR. AND MRS. RUDOLPH SCHNAIDT

Rudolph Schnaidt son of George and Karolina (nee Burkhardt) Schnaidt was born
October 8, 1906 about 20 miles south of Beulah. Rudolph can well remember when
Fairington was the only store close by. In 1914 he helped build the first
church in town--Zion Lutheran Church.

Rudolph started school 3 miles north of Beulah. He attended school at Eureka,
South Dakota from 1921 to 1924. Due to the death of his Father in 1924, he went
back to help his Mother with the farm.

On July 21, 1929 he married Anna Heth daughter of Wilhelm and Lydia (nee
Kruger) Heth. She was born on April 2, 1910 in the Expansion area north of
Hazen. She received her education in rural schools.

Mr. and Mrs. Schnaidt lived on a farm Southwest of Beulah 4 months, then moved
5 miles west of Beulah. Due to drought and grasshoppers, and the end result
being poor crops, they moved into Beulah in 1937.

Rudolph is a Star Route Mail Carrier since 1934, in addition to the route he
has been clerking in grocery stores for 9 years; the last 6 years for Galloway
Super Valu.

Mr. and Mrs. Schnaidt have a family of five children, they are: Marvin,
employed at the M.D.U. plant in Beulah; Rev. Reuben G. Schnaidt, assistant
pastor Huron, South Dakota; Arlene (Mrs. Merlin Orth) on a dairy farm, Waverly,
Iowa; Delmer, a student at Wartburg College, Waverly, Iowa; and Janet at home.

Mr. and Mrs. Schnaidt are faithful church members of Zion Lutheran Church.

THE LUDWIG SAILER FAMILY

        Ludwig and Salome

Ludwig Sailer, the son of Mattheis Sailer and his wife Barbara, nee Deltzer,
was born at Johannestal in South Russia, in 1883. In 1902 he came with his
parents to America, first to South Dakota; within six weeks the family moved to
Mercer County, North Dakota.

Mrs. Ludwig Sailer, the former Salome Erdmann, was born in the province of
Bessarabia, South Russia, on May 13, 1891. Her parents were John and Justina
Erdmann (nee Schwitz). She came to America in 1907 at the age of sixteen, and
spent thirty sea-sick days aboard ship. Her first home here was the sod house
built by her brother John, with cloth tacked in the ceiling to prevent dirt
from falling and the wooden doorway the only place to keep dry during a
rainstorm. Mrs. Sailer worked at various farms in the Golden Valley area until
being married to Ludwig Sailer on January 1, 1911. The wedding was performed by
a Reverend Sumanski in the only church in the area--a Reformed Church; The
Lutheran Church in Beulah was built a short time later.

Mr. and Mrs. Sailer lived in Mercer County before Beulah was established,
having homesteaded two quarters of land directly north of the present Highway
#7 and about three miles from Beulah. In reminiscing, Mrs. Sailer often recalls
the coming of the railroad, the early Beulah business places, the fierce
blizzards, and the trips to Golden Valley to visit relatives. They always
remained overnight because of the great "traveling" distance. During cold
weather a trusty buffalo robe covered the car's radiator. When it was stolen,
it was difficult to find an efficient substitute for it.

When the Knife River Coal Mining Company purchased their land in 1932, the
Sailers bought one of the well-established farms in the area--one originally
homesteaded by John Henderson and located about 1 1/2 miles west of Beulah.
Misfortune struck in a few years when the large two-story home burned. A new
home was soon built and recently remodeled by the present owners of the farm.

Mr. Sailer died December 20, 1942. Mrs. Sailer bought the former Gotthold
Sailer residence and moved to Beulah in 1945.

Ten children were born to the couple, of whom one (Selma) died in infancy.
Otto, married to the former Dolores Weigum, lives in Beulah where he is foreman
for a firm constructing Butler Buildings. His wife is employed at the Beulah
Nursing Home. The couple have two children--Arlo and Arlette.

In 1957, Ludwig bought the home farm and is presently residing there. His wife,
the former Myrtle Melby, taught in the Beulah School from 1939-1942. She
returned to teaching nine years ago and has been employed by the Zap School
each year. Their children are Glenn, a student at Wahpeton State School of
Science, Gretchen, Deborah, Loren, and Ames, all at home.

Mrs. William Wahler (Hertha) lives in Lakewood, California. Mr. Wahler is
engaged in construction work. Their three daughters--Arliss, Rose Marie, and
Selma--are married and living in California. William, Jr. is a college student.

Adolph and his wife Esther, nee Singer, reside on their farm fifteen miles
southeast of Beulah with their daughter, Carla Jean. She is attending a rural
Oliver County school. Their home was recently newly-built.

Jacob, his wife Lilly, nee Schock, and their two children, Sidney and Patricia
Jane, live on their farm six miles north of Beulah. Mrs. Sailer is employed
part-time at Galloway's Super-Valu in Beulah. The children both attend Beulah
School.

Reuben, employed by the Knife River Coal Mining Company for several years,
makes his home in Beulah with his mother.

Mrs. Gerald Hughes (Lorraine) is residing in Fort Scott, Kansas, in a new home
recently constructed by her husband whose occupation is that of a brick-layer.
Their three children are Terry Lee, Sheryl, and Robert.

Mrs. Clayton Schramm (Arvada) lives in Bismarck where they have purchased a new
home and where Mr. Schramm is employed by Montana-Dakota Utilities. They have
three children--David, Susan, and Steven.

The youngest of the children, Florence, is a teacher. She attended Valley City
State College, taught one year in a rural Beulah school and one year in Great
Falls, Montana, before returning to college to earn her Bachelor of Science
Degree in Education. Since then she has taught high school English in Bowman
and in Oakes, North Dakota. At the present she is teaching English in the
Hughes Junior High School. Bismarck, North Dakota.

THE YEAR OF 1919

October -- payroll at Mine per day is $450.00, and the average output of coal was
265 tons per day.

Fall -- started building on the new brick school house -- the first brick
business place was built by Ted. Muhlhauser.

June -- night school classes started -- eighteen enrolled.

THE YEAR OF 1921

March 4 -- the following ad appeared--Buy it in Beulah whether its shoes, booze
or snoose.

September -- the building of the houses on Chaffee Row began.

May 3 -- rural mail delivery for north of Belilah began.

THE JOHN SAILER FAMILY HISTORY

        Herbert, Karl, John Sr., Emil, Mrs. John Sailer, Edwin, Andrew and
        Anna. Front: Richard and Oscar.

Mr. John Sailer was born in Johannestahl S. Russia, on November 22, 1879, the
son of Mattheis and Barbara (nee Deltzer) Sailer. In 1901 two of the Sailer
brothers, John and Jacob left for America. They arrived at South Dakota where
they worked for about a year. In 1902 the two brothers were joined by their
parents and the rest of the family. They all migrated to Mercer County the same
year. John filed a homestead claim on a 160 acres of land 5 miles northeast of
Beulah (the P.O. was Farrington at that time).

Magdalene Kuntz was born near New Salem on January 23, 1888. The year before
(1887) the John Kuntz's had immigrated to America settling for a year at New
Salem. In 1888 they moved to Krem where they took up a homestead. There were 12
children in the Kuntz family.

Magdalene and John Sailer were married in 1905 in the Krem Lutheran Church by
Pastor Engler. They moved onto John's homestead. It was here that all of the
Sailer children were born and reared in the Lutheran faith. John recognized the
need for a Lutheran Church in the newly formed town of Beulah; therefore he was
one of the organizers and Charter Members of the Zion Lutheran Church (1914).

John and Magdalene lived on the Sailer farm (now known as the Emil Sailer farm)
until Mr. Sailers death on August 23, 1932. Mrs. Sailer farmed with her sons
until 1937 when she remarried and moved to Lodi, California.

The John Sailers had one daughter and seven sons: Anna, Mrs. Jack Oster of
Hazen; Herbert of Hazen (married Louise Merkel); Carl of Hazen (married Bertha
Becker); Emil of Beulah (married Olga Koehler); Edwin of Hazen (married Helen
Becker); Andrew of Beulah (married Florence Bates); Oscar of Bismarck (married
Lillian Lemrick); and Richard of Lodi, California (married Marcella La
Fabiera--A French war-bride). There are 23 grandchildren and 17
great-grandchildren.

Magdalene makes her home in Lodi, California. She is married to Mike Bertch.

        KARL AND CHRISTINA SAILER

The above pictures were taken of Karl and Christina Sailer about 50 years ago.

Karl Sailer was born April 28, 1890, at Johannestahl, South Russia to Mattheis
Sailer and wife Barbara, nee Deltzer. At the age of 12 he accompanied his
parents, brothers and sisters to America settling for a short time in South
Dakota. They then moved to Mercer County--homesteading about four miles north
of Beulah.

On September 17, 1912 he married Christina Erdman. Christina was born January
12, 1895. She came from Russia with her mother in 1907. She lived in the Golden
Valley vicinity until she married.

After they were married the Karl Sailers settled on a farm five miles north of
Beulah. Together they weathered the hardships of the early farming days, the
days of the depression and drought. It was on this farm that all of their
children were born, and raised. They lived here until 1945 when, due to Karl's
poor health they were forced to retire. They bought a home and moved into
Beulah. Christ, their oldest son, took over the management of the farm.

The Karl Sailers were members of the Zion Lutheran Church of which Christina is
still a member. Mr. Sailer passed away on October 3, 1946 at the age 56.

To this union were born ten children: Emma of Grafton, N. D. Christ married
Norman Reiner in 1945. They are the parents of three children; Rodney (age 14),
Dean (age 11) and Pamela (age 4).

Rose passed away at the age of twenty.

Walter, who was a member of the U. S. Navy during World War II, lives on a farm
near Zap. He is single.

Linda married Wm. Oberlander in 1942. They live in Hazen. Their children are;
Clarence, Audrey, Rose, and Rhonda. Hulda married Norman Fuchs in 1947. They
live in Zap and have six children namely; Nona, Perry, Gail, Sammy, Susan and
Christy.

Henry graduated from the Beulah High School in 1946--after which he served a
hitch in the U.S. Navy. He is married to the former Sylvia Miller of New York.
They make their home at Salt Lake City, Utah. Their four children are; Allen,
Marilyn, Billy and Carol.

Ernest and his daughter, Julie, make their home with Mrs. Sailer at Beulah

        Receptionist, La Vonne, Mrs. Dennis Kirchmeier

Dr. J. A. Power is a native of Crosby, North Dakota. He received his early
education in Crosby and graduated from the University of North Dakota in 1950.
He then attended Southern College of Optometry, Memphis, Tennessee and in June
of 1953 received the degree of Doctor of Optometry. In the fall of 1953 Dr.
Power opened his practice in Beulah.

He is married to the former Phyllis Kjerstad of Grand Forks, North Dakota. They
have four daughters, Paula, Kathryn, Elizabeth and Holly.

        Dr. Power with a young patient.

THE JOHN SCHULZ FAMILY

        Standing--Left to Right: Edward, Ferdinand, Joe, Paul, Carl, and
        William. Sitting--Left to Right: Selma, Herbert, John (father),
        Julia (mother), Hertha and Hilda.

My late husband, John Schulz, was born in South Russia on January 3, 1878, to
Gottfried and Louisa, nee Steht, Schulz. Here he received his education and
grew into manhood. He served for four years in the Russian Army. While he was
in the army his parents and the rest of his family immigrated to America. In
1905 John was discharged from service, he went home and married Juliana Koenig,
the daughter of Ferdinand and Christina, nee Roder, Koenig. Her birthdate was
January 7, 1884.

In 1906 he and his wife came from the "old country" and settled on a homestead
6 1/2 miles south of Beulah. These were the times that John always remembered
as the hardworking days of plowing and harvesting fields with horses. This
union was blessed with seven boys; Ed, Ferdinand, Joseph, Paul, Carl, William,
and Herbert. On June 10, 1923, Mrs. John Schulz passed away, leaving behind her
seven motherless sons. Herbert, the youngest, was only a few months old at the
time of her death. On her death-bed her last request was, that this small baby
be given to her dear sister, (Mrs. Edward Hummel of Medicine Hat, Alberta,
Canada) to raise as her own. Being a man of his word, John kept the promise he
had made to his dying wife, although as time went on, it proved to be one of
the hardest promises John ever had to keep. Herbert grew to manhood in Canada.
He saw his father and brothers for the first time in 1946, when his father sent
for him (the above picture was taken at this reunion).

John tried his best to be both mother and father to his boys. After his field
work was done he had to spend many hours washing clothes, baking bread, and
cooking for his family of growing boys. The way friends and neighbors pitched
in and helped him in so many ways, was deeply appreciated and never forgotten.

On March 12, 1924, John married me, Mrs. Juliana Mann. I was born on October
26, 1895 in Kronthal, S. Russia, the daughter of Michael and Philipina, nee
Wagner, Koehler. I had been left a widow with two little girls, Hertha and
Hilda Mann. We had been living on a farm north of Halliday, when my husband,
John Mann, passed away on November 18, 1918. On March 12, 1924 I married John
Schulz, and my girls and I moved to the Schulz farm. Here I tried to be a good
mother to the six Schulz boys. On March 5, 1925, we were blessed with a
daughter, Selma. Our children grew up together as one family. These were good
and bad years, as life was a constant struggle, but daily prayers and a strong
faith in God made our life on the North Dakota prairies much  easier.

After the children were grown up, married or out on their own, we felt that it
was time to retire, so in 1946 we moved into Beulah. The past was now only a
memory. Here it was always a pleasure to have our children come and visit us.
Our grandchildren always gave us a great deal of enjoyment. Then on November
21, 1954, John passed away, after a few months of illness, leaving me again a
widow. I have always been proud of my family of seven step-sons and three
daughters, they are: Edward married Rose Klundt, has a family of six sons and
one daughter. He farms north of Beulah and has served as a County Commissioner
of Mercer County for the last 10 years. Ferdinand married Ava Beuhl, he has a
daughter and 1 step-daughter. They live at Auburn, Washington, where he is
employed as a mechanic. Joe married Mary Ellen Emig (now deceased) has one
daughter. He is now married to Mrs. Hilda Pfenning Eid and is engaged in
farming.

Paul married Wanda O'Dell and has 2 children and 2 step-children. He is part
owner of the Home Floor Co. at St. Paul, Minnesota. Carl is an ordained
Lutheran Pastor. He married Germaine Brockhaus, has 2 children and they live at
Colfax, Washington.

William married Lenora Boeckel and they have 4 children. He is employed by the
City of Beulah, as a maintainance man and part-time marshal.

Herbert married Ella Summerfelt and operates a farm at Brighton, Alberta,
Canada. They have two children. Hertha, Mrs. Emil Maas lives on a farm north of
Hazen and has one daughter.

Hilda, Mrs. Alfred Rahn has one son and lives on a farm south of Hazen.

Sally, Mrs. Ted Wentz has two children and during the summer months they live
on their farm north of Dodge.

Since my husband's death I have been making my home with my youngest daughter,
Sally. During the 9 months of the school year, the Wentz's and I live in
Beulah. I am a member of the Zion Lutheran Church, the church my husband and I
attended during the years of our marriage.

THE ERICK AND ANNA STROM HISTORY

Erick Strom was born on September 16, 1857 in Orsa, Sweden. In 1877, after
having served in the Swedish Army, he married Anna Peterson Hanson, a young
widow with two small daughters. In 1880 they immigrated to America and settled
at Cambridge, Minnesota. Here one daughter Anna married Willie Erickson and
Christine married Harvey Erickson, now of Beulah. Josephine and Elphie were
born at Cambridge. Mrs. Strom died on March 13, 1898.

In 1903 Erick made a trip to Sweden to bring his mother to America. In the fall
of 1903 he married again, a Swedish girl, Anna Nyquist, whom he had met
recently. Two of their seven children were born at Cambridge, Victor and
Sylvia. In April 1906 they emigrated to N. Dak. homesteading 25 miles north of
Glen Ullin on Sec. 8-142-87, one mile north of Evans Post Office. The children
were reared here on the prairie, and sent three miles to the Aplin Church for
Sunday School. Church Services were often held in the Strom home. Erick Strom
was a charter member of the Aplin Telephone Co. Five children were born on the
homestead; Phoebe, Elvera, Marvin, Evelyn and Edgar. Christine and Josephine
had homesteaded nearby. Erick Strom died on August 27, 1924.

Josephine married Charles Pennington and they had 11 grown children. Elphie has
5 children, all married, he lives in Payette, Idaho. Victor married Edna
Adamson in 1927, they live one mile north of the homestead, and have four
children, all married. Sylvia married Basil Osborne in 1934, they have 3
daughters and live in Lodi, Calif. Phoebe married Lloyd Schmid, formerly of
Beulah in 1941, they have three children and live in Moses Lake, Wash. Elvera
married George Stohl, formerly of Beulah, in 1946, they have 2 daughters and
live in Olympia, Wash. Marvin married Mabel Beck in 1940, they have 3 sons and
live 2 miles south of the homestead. Evelyn married James Kruchoski in 1943,
they have three children and live in Bremerton, Wash. Edgar married Laurie
Wiebe in 1945 and lives on the Strom homestead, they have 3 children. Mrs.
Strom died on Feb. 3, 1940.

A highlight of the Strom children was a family reunion held in June, 1960, the
first time in 36 years, that all were together. It took place at Beulah on the
homeplace. Josephine Strom Pennington passed away in October of that year.

All of the children, except Josephine, attended the country school in Burt
district, about a mile and a half from home. The girls all went on to become
professional school teachers. They have a total of 85 years of teaching since
1924.

At the present time there are eight living children, thirty-seven
grandchildren, forty-three great grandchildren and twenty-seven great, great
grandchildren.

MR. AND MRS. GOTTLIEB KLAUDT

Gottlieb Klaudt was born on January 17, 1870 in Paulsdale, Russia to Daniel and
Louisa Klaudt. His father died when he was only three years old so he was
raised by relatives. They brought him with them when they came to America in
1884. He had been a Mercer County resident since the age of fourteen.

Katherina Klaudt was born in Odessa, S. Russia on December 23, 1872 to Jacob
and Maria Mulke Becker. She came to the U.S. in 1889 with her parents who
settled in the Menno, S. Dakota area. Three of her half-brothers are still
living; Carl Becker of Bismarck, Edward Becker of Mandan, and Emanuel Becker of
Lodi.

Gottlieb and Katherina Klaudt were married at Menno, S. Dak., in October, 1891.
From 1891 to 1905 the Klaudts lived at Menno and Gackle. They moved back to
Mercer County in 1905 where they bought three quarters of land north of Beulah
for $8. an acre. Later on they bought one more quarter. Here the Klaudt's
raised their family of twelve children (the last six were born on this farm).
The Klaudts were a very religious couple and gave their children a wholesome
Christian upbringing. The church meant a great deal to them. The German Baptist
Church actually had some of it's first services in the Klaudt farm home. They
were instrumental in the organization of the Immanuel Baptist Church of Beulah,
of which they were Charter Members. They were active and faithful members of
this church until their deaths. In 1936 the Klaudts rented out their farm land
and moved into Beulah, where they lived until Gottlieb died on Angust 21, 1950.
After the death of her husband, Mrs. Klaudt moved to Hazen. She lived in a new,
modern trailer home which was parked near her son Arthur's house. In 1955 she
moved to the Joachim Memorial Home at Beulah, where she lived the remaining
years of her life. She died on March 20, 1957.

The union of Gottlieb and Katharina Klaudt was blessed with fourteen children,
two of whom died as infants. The twelve that reached adulthood are: Robert of
Yakima, was born on October 14, 1892, died May, 1960; John of Salem,
Oregon--born August 1895; Jacob of Sacramento, Calif.--born Feb. 27, 1897--died
1961; Theodore of Moses Lake, Wash.--born May 18, 1900--died January 1957;
Emil of Oswego, Oregon--born October 21, 1902; Kathryn Klaudt Hudson, of
Lewiston, Mont.--born Aug. 19, 1905; Arthur of Hazen, N. D.--born March 1,
1907; Rev. Reinhold Klaudt of Daraville, Georgia--born January 20, 1908;
Pauline of Salem, Oregon--born March 16, 1910; Daniel of Torrance
California--born July 2, 1911; Frieda Klaudt Schott of Kulm, N. Dak.--born
January 23, 1914; and Christ of Omaha, Nebraska--born January 29, 1916.

There are fifty-one grandchildren and eighty-nine great-grandchildren.

        Standing--Left to right: Viola, Alta, Delores, Esther and Adeline.
        Sitting--Left to right: John Tesky, Betty Mae, Lydia Tesky.

JOHN AND LYDIA TESKY

John was born in Bon Homme County, S. Dak. on Dec. 6, 1894 to Joseph and
Katharina, nee Wesser, Tesky. The Joseph Teskys had 7 sons and 2 daughters,
Gottlieb, Karl, Christina, (Mrs. John Pischel), Jacob, Fred, John, Henry,
Katie, (Mrs. Emil Permann), and Andrew. Fred, John, Henry, Katie and Andrew are
still living. In April of 1900 John moved, with his parents, to Mercer County,
where his father took up a homestead 8 mi. north of Beulah. This homestead is
still part of the present John Tesky farm.

John was inducted into the U.S. Army in 1918 and received his Honorable
Discharge in 1919. He then returned to the family homestead, which he continued
to farm until the Teskys retired in 1961.

Lydia Tesky was born on March 2, 1897 in Bon Homme County, S. Dak. to Michael
and Katharina, nee Huber, Guthmiller. Lydia's mother passed away when she was
only 14 yrs. of age, and being the oldest girl in the family the duty of caring
for her father and raising her 5 brothers and 2 sisters fell upon her young
shoulders. This she did until she married at the age of 22.

Lydia and John were married on June 22, 1919 in the Reformed Church at Tripp,
S. Dak. by Rev. U. Zogg. To this union were born 9 children; Viola (Mrs. Art
Heth), Alta (Mrs. Jack Reinhardt), Esther (Mrs. Ted Kirchmeier), Adeline (Mrs.
Arthur Ziemann), Delores (Mrs. Elmer Renz), Betty Mae (Mrs. Fred Hausauer).
Three children died in infancy. They have 16 grandchildren and 2 great
grandchildren. They still own the original Tesky Homestead although they no
longer do the farming. They have resided in Beulah since 1961. They are members
of the Zion Lutheran Church. John has been a member of the Beulah American
Legion for 34 years.

GEORGE AND ANN SCHNAIDT

George Schnaidt was born to John Schnaidt and Rosina, nee Huber, on Feb. 23,
1908 in Eureka, S. Dak. When he was a few months old, his parents moved to a
farm 20 miles southwest of Beulah.

On March 27, 1932 George was united in marriage to Anna C. Boepple, daughter of
the late George Boepple and Katherine, nee Goetz, Stanton, N. Dak. Mrs.
Schnaidt attended school in Stanton and graduated in 1930. She attended Valley
City Normal for a term and then taught rural schools for three years. Her
father, Mr. Boepple, was a band leader in several of the communities in the
surrounding area.

Mrs. Schnaidt taught school north of Beulah the first winter of their marriage.
They moved to Beulah in the spring of 1933.

Mr. Schnaidt was employed by the Knife River Coal Mining Co. on Nov. 1, 1933.
He has worked there ever since, except for five months when they lived at
Everett, Washington. He is Tipple foreman at the Beulah mine.

The Schnaidts have two children, Joyce Joanne and Lloyd George. Joyce is
married to John Schlecht. John is employed by the N.P.R.R. on pickup and
delivery at Billings, Montana. They have two children, Ranae Ann, 10 years old,
and Cindy Lu, 6 years old.

Lloyd graduated from The School of Pharmacy of N.D.S.U. in Fargo in 1960. He is
a Pharmacist at the Service Drug Store at Dickinson, N. Dak. He is married to
Marleen Herman.

George and Ann Schnaidt are members of the Zion Lutheran Church, where Ann has
served as a Sunday School teacher for many years.

George has two brothers, John of Hebron and Jacob of Bismarck. His four sisters
are: Mrs. Hugo Auwinger, Mrs. Otto Reiner, and Mrs. Lydia Zuern all of Hazen
and Mrs. Bill Hilz of Beulah.

Ann has three sisters, Mrs. Albert Schutz, Sturgis, S. D., Mrs. Joe Brasseur,
Coeur D'Alene; and Mrs. August Neuberger, Beulah, N. Dak. Two brothers and two
sisters are deceased.

MR. AND MRS. C. E. PETERSON

Carl E. Peterson, son of Mr. and Mrs. Charles O. Peterson was born in 1884 in
Dakota Territory near Aberdeen. As a young man he homesteaded. He proved up his
homestead in 1910 and the same year married Lena Aaker of Flandreau, who had
also homesteaded.

They had one son, Royal, when they came to Killdeer, N. D. Here their second
son was born in March of 1915. This son was the first child born in the new
town of Killdeer and was christened Dale Killdeer to commemorate this fact.
From Killdeer, Petersons moved to Hazen where "Pete" managed the Mandan
Mercantile lumber yard and had some farming interests. Number III son Celon
Aaker Peterson was born here. While residents of Hazen, Mr. Peterson became a
member of the Masonic Lodge and Mrs. Peterson of the Eastern Star.

In 1929 Lena and "Pete" with their 3 boys moved to Beulah, where Mr. Peterson,
generally known as "Mandan Pete" managed the Mandan Mercantile lumber yard in
Beulah for 16 years, and Mrs. Peterson managed the hotel which they purchased.

Mr. Peterson was elected to the village council in 1932, a position he held
until his resignation in 1947. At the time of his resignation, the town's
indebtedness had been retired. "Pete" in a news story at that time, outlined
some of the progress he saw for Beulah in the near future, including a new City
Hall, better street lighting, paved streets and better roads, all of which are
realities today.

Their children are: Royal, married to Hazel Newton. They have two children, a
girl and a boy. Royal is finance officer for Boeing Minute Man program at
Minot, N. D. Dale Killdeer is married to Virginia Sarkisian. They have one
daughter, Wendy. Active duty in World War II included Casablanca, Battle of the
Phillipines, South China Sea Strikes, Iwo Jima and Japan, for which he was
awarded the Distinguished Flying Cross and two Air Medals. At present, he is
Commander of the assault carrier USS Guadalcanal, with the rank of Captain.
Celon married Echo June Molton. They have two children. Celon served in World
War II. He was wounded in Sicily. He came out of the war with rank of Major. He
practices dentistry at Pacific Palisades, California.

In 1947 Mr. Peterson resigned his position as manager of the lumber company.
They moved to Everett, Wash. where they now reside.

MARY AND MIKE HELD

Mr. and Mrs. Mike Held, daughter Carol and son Lucas came to Beulah December
30, 1928 from Glen Ullin, Mandan and Hazen where they had previously lived that
year.

Mike was associated with the Marshall-Malaise Hardware until 1932 when he
transferred to the Frank Pitzer General Store. He stayed there after Joe
Charvat purchased the store until 1939, when he went into business for himself,
opening a grocery store under the name of "Mike's Grocery." It was later
changed to "Mike's Jack & Jill." In January of 1960 he sold his business to
George and Selma Koppi.

Mike was then employed as City Auditor until his resignation in June 1963, when
plans were made to move to California. Mike also served on various active
Community affairs and the City Board and Park Board. He was also active in the
St. Joseph's Church organizations. In Hazen, Mike was employed in the Schulz
Hardware store, as well as the Daffinrud Hardware Store. He later worked in the
Valancey Hardware Store in Mandan. In Glen Ullin, he was with the Standard Oil
Company, but went back to the hardware business in Beulah.

Mary taught school in Hazen for four years. Mary was also active in all
community affairs, and is a charter member of the Beulah Woman's Federated
Club. She became Postmaster in March 1936, and served all County and State
offices in the National Association of Posmmasters. She recently received a
plaque in recognition of her twenty-five years of service in the County and
City for the March of Dimes.

Carol received her grade and high school education in Beulah; attended college
in Fargo and served two years in the Navy as a WAVE. She married Sam Hoersch in
Beulah, February 1950. They have four daughters.

Lucas also received his grade and high school education in Beulah; served two
years in the Navy, and was graduated from St. Thomas College in St. Paul,
Minnesota. He married LaVerne Hall in Prescott, Arizona in  January 1953. They
have three sons and four daughters.

Both Carol and Lucas and their families now live in San Diego, which was the
reason for their parents move to California.

        Ernie and Dennis

In 1927 the Peavey Co. Lumber Service was owned by the Mandan Mercantile Co.
The Manager was Tony Kemper, now deceased. Second man was Peter Malsom. At that
time the lumber yard handled a line of John Deere machinery along with building
materials.

In 1928 Peter Malsom was transferred to the Mandan Mercantile Co. at
Gladstone, North Dakota. At this time (1929) C. E. Peterson (Mandan Pete) came
to take over the management of the yard. He retired in 1945.

Later when Occident Lumber Co. bought the yards and Pete Malsom returned to
Beulah as manager. Pete was transferred to Halliday in 1960 where he manages
the Peavey Co. Lumber Service.

In January 1961 Ernest L. Ohlhauser took over as manager of the Occident Lumber
Co. of Beulah. Ernest had started as a helper in December 1958 in the Occident
Elevator and Lumber Co. at Cleveland, N. D. His present helper is Dennis Tahran
who came to us from the yards at Cleveland in 1962.

The Occident Lumber Co. was changed over to the Peavey Company Lumber Service,
on July 1, 1963. The Peavey Company is proud to be a part of this community and
Congratulates Beulah on its 50th Anniversary.

Beulah, North Dakota

February 29, 1964

L. F. Temme, also known as Louis F. Temme, has a commercial education, from
Dakota Business College of Fargo, North Dakota, in Commerce and Banking.

He has been a resident of Beulah, since November 23, 1916, except from March
29, 1918 to March 11, 1919, while in Service during World War I.

He has been in business since April 1919, Real Estate, Insurance, Farm and City
Rentals. He has serviced farm rentals in Mercer, Oliver, Dunn, Morton, Grant,
Sioux, Billings,Burleigh and McKenzie Counties. His present office was built in
September 1921.

        This picture was taken in 1922 of L. F. Temme and inside of his
        office. He sitting at his desk and Chas. Herman, with cap on, his
        customer seated to his right.

He was married to Helga S. Melsted of Mountain, North Dakota, on October 24,
1936. They had one daughter deceased. He served in the capacity as Director and
Vice President of the German State Bank Beulah, from July 1931 to March 31,
1938. Served as school clerk of Beulah School District No. 27, for 11 years.
Served on the Village Board. Was Village and City Treasurer, for 30 years. Held
various offices in civic organizations. Member of Beulah Fire Department since
August 20, 1920 to the present time.

        This picture was taken on June 25, 1932 of L. F. Temme and inside of
        his office with his stenographer, Frances Moore.

LOUIS F. TEMME

        Louis F. Temme, was born in Okawville, Illinois. Came to New Salem,
        North Dakota, on Sept. 10, 1910. Helga S. Temme, was born in Mountain,
        North Dakota. She taught school for twelve years, before her marriage.

Louis and Helga, his wife, have lived in their present home since marriage to
the present time. They are members of The American Zion Lutheran Church of
Beulah. He served as church treasurer; was on the Building Committee, when the
present brick church building was built in 1940. He is also serving on the
1963-1964 Church Building Committee, of the new church building under
construction at present time.

He bought the Old Hospital Building in 1947, in order to keep a hospital in
Beulah. This hospital was closed down September 1, 1949, after Hazen had opened
up their hospital in the early spring of 1949. He spent a lot of time to raise
money for the new hospital. He bought all of Schmidt's Addition and surveyed
this into lots and donated the present Nursing Home site to the Hospital
Association, and sold the balance to the Hospital Association for cost. The new
hospital building was started in the spring of 1950. It was never opened for a
hospital. In 1961 it was sold to the Beulah Community Nursing Home Association,
for actual cost and the lots in 1963 for actual cost, except Block 3 & 8 was
sold to Zion Lutheran Church for actual cost.

This history is put in for the 50th Anniversary of Beulah, North Dakota, to be
held on June 18, 19, 20, 1964.
